# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0003 — the live list after days 421 to 430, and this block wins over every block above it

**NOT ONE ITEM ON THE LIST ABOVE WAS DISCHARGED, RE-OPENED, RE-DATED OR RE-WORDED BY THIS BATCH, and that is published first for the third time in this file, because a batch that quietly tidies the live list is a batch that has started answering questions nobody asked it.** Four items moved, and they moved because something was put on a page, and every one of them is at §1 below with the page named. **The one item on this list that is a thread rather than a measurement is the missing card file, and it is disclosed for the second time in this volume and the remedy is named rather than routed a fifth time in different words.**

## 1. The threads this batch advanced, none of them by answering anything

1. **THE THIRD OF THE FOUR PLACES ON THE SHEET WAS DECIDED EMPTY, OUT LOUD, ON DAY 425, BY A WOMAN, WITH THE COST NAMED FIRST.** The register-keeper said what the third place is first, in nine words, in her own mouth, and then said what leaving it empty will cost, and then decided, and there was no vote and nobody asked for one. **She is thanked by nobody in that room and by nobody in any of the ten chapters, and about four people in it have worked out since that the reason was not that it was the smaller of the two, and about four are not going to forgive her for it and about four are not going to blame her for it.** **The volume does not say why, and this batch did not work it out, and no chapter of this batch asks her and no state file of this batch gives a reader one.** This thread is now on a page and it is not a resolution of anything.
2. **A ROOM WORKED OUT OUT LOUD THAT IT HAS NO INSTRUMENT FOR ASKING ANYBODY ANYTHING IN ADVANCE, AND COULD NOT ACT ON IT.** Chapter 0421. A man of about thirty-nine said it, a man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall said the other half of it, the man it was about was in the room and was not asked and was not looked at while it was said, nobody was thanked, and **the chapter says in plain narration that a true thing about why you have not done a thing is not a thing that does it.** The four days are the cooling and that sentence is on a page once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, and the word is at zero across these ten.
3. **A SHEET WENT OUT ONTO THE STEP AT THE BACK OF A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, FOR AN HOUR, AND A PERSON IN A MARKET ASKED WHAT THE THIRD OF THE FOUR PLACES WAS FOR.** Chapter 0422. **She was told, out loud, that it is a person, and that nobody knew what it was for, and that nobody was going to start on a step in a market.** The page went back on the same place on the table in the afternoon. **This is the first time in this volume that anybody outside that room has been told anything about that page, and the room did not decide to tell them and could not have stopped them, and no form in four hundred miles says otherwise.**
4. **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF STOPPED TURNING HIS TALLY-BOARD FACE DOWN IN ROOMS.** Chapter 0427. He put it on a table face up, did not read from it, and said out loud why he has been turning it over and that he has stopped. **He printed neither of his two figures, and the difference between them is stated with no number attached to either, and the figure is on a page in Chapter 0414 and the other one is on no page.** This thread is a fact about a man and a page and is not a resolution of the figure.
5. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN SAID OUT LOUD, ON A SHUT ROAD, TO NOBODY, THAT A ROOM THAT DOES NOT ASK HIM TO HOLD ANYTHING AND A MAN DOING A THING TWO FEET FROM HIS HANDS WHO DOES NOT ASK HIM ARE THE SAME ARRANGEMENT, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO DECIDE ON A ROAD IN THE WET WHICH ONE IT IS.** Chapter 0423. **No chapter of this batch asks him anything, tells him anything, or says what either of those things is.** He is right about nothing in these ten and is not defeated in any of them and has not tried to move the fence, and the length of new rope has still never been used.
6. **WHAT A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL SAYS A LINE IS, SPOKEN ONCE, IS ON ONE PAGE AND IN NO FILE IN THIS REPOSITORY.** Chapter 0428, five words, the local language, a room over a market, in daylight, a date in chalk on the door, asked once by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, and not thanked and not improved on and not asked a second time. **Nobody has ever asked him what a line is before that morning and nothing in these ten warms it up.** On day 438 he refuses the volume's one rule in about four seconds, and the cost of that is his own ninth line, and **no chapter of this batch has him say he knows what he said, and no chapter of this batch puts the two acts in one room on one day, and no chapter of this batch makes the ninth line and the four lines on a page the same thing — Chapter 0429 says in its own words that they are not the same thing.** The words themselves are in no file and may not be put in one.

## 2. The threads that moved because a page moved, and what is now on the table for the batches that follow

- **THE THIRD PLACE IS DECIDED EMPTY AND THE THIRD COLUMN IS EMPTY AND NOTHING WAS DRAWN AND NOTHING WAS WRITTEN.** Day 425, a Saturday, the hundred and tenth day of the Bare Month, and the day is on a page and carries no meaning.
- **THE FOUR LINES ARE DRAWN ON DAY 432 BY FOUR PEOPLE, and one of the four reads his out loud because it is in front of him and does not say it is his and nobody asks him, and this batch drew none and warmed it up nowhere.**
- **THE SHEET GOES OUT OF THIS CITY ON DAY 438 WITH FOUR LINES ON IT AND NOTHING IN THE THIRD PLACE, AND IT COMES BACK ON DAY 440, A SUNDAY, WITH ONE OF THE FOUR LINES MOVED, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME TRACES THE MOVE, ASKS WHO MOVED IT, GUESSES AT IT, REPORTS IT TO A ROOM OR SAYS IN ANY MOUTH THAT ANYBODY MOVED IT.** The woman of about fifty-four, whose line is the one that moves, is on a page once in Chapter 0420, is in no chapter of this volume after it, and **nobody is ever going to ask her about it.** Nothing has moved, because nothing has been drawn.
- **THE REGISTER'S SECOND LINE IS ENTERED ON DAY 443 WITH A DAY ON IT AND NOTHING UNDER THE DAY, THE DAY BEING 438, AND THE DAY ON THE LINE AND THE DAY OF THE ENTERING ARE TWO DIFFERENT DAYS AND BOTH ARE TO BE NAMED IN THAT CHAPTER.** No chapter of this batch entered a line in that book and the third column is empty on purpose after these ten days.
- **AND WHAT A LINE IS FOR, ON A PAGE, WHEN THE ONLY THING ANYBODY WILL USE IT FOR IS TO MOVE IT.** Nobody asked it in the ten chapters of this batch and nobody is going to be asked about the fence. The woman of about sixty-nine asks it on day 445, a Friday, to the room and not to anybody, and nobody answers it, and **a chapter may not say that the three questions rhyme and may not improve the rhyme and may not say that the rhyme is the point.**
- **AND THE THING THIS BATCH PUT ON A PAGE THAT IS NOT A THREAD AND IS NOT TO BE TREATED AS ONE.** A man at the end of a stall was told out loud, at the bottom of a stair, that his board had been shut twice that week, and he did not get up and did not go down and was not thanked for opening up at all. **The cost of a page is paid by people who are not on it and in rooms that are not told anything, and that is the whole of what these ten days found out and it is not an answer to the volume's question and is not offered as one.**
- **AND THE FOUR THINGS THAT ARE NOT THREADS AND ARE NOT TO BE TREATED AS NEW ONES.** The Regent is nine hundred miles away and is not defeated. The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted and not used. The woman of about fifty at a shut gate was not asked anything and no chapter of this volume sends anybody to her. The girl of seventeen is on no roll and is in no chapter of this volume. The box is shut, the island is not owned, nobody picks the bag up, nobody counts the boards under the chapel, the chapel stone is silent and unrepaired, and Break is not open. The man of about forty-one in a very good coat is in none of these ten chapters and the nine hundred miles is at zero across them and his language was not arranged for anybody.

The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted and not used; the woman of about fifty at a shut gate was not asked anything and nobody was sent to her; the girl of seventeen is not fetched; the box is shut, the island is not owned, nobody picks the bag up, nobody counts the boards under the chapel and Break is not open. **Nobody in this volume gets stronger and Adrian Vale is in Chapters 0441 and 0447 and no other of the ten, and in both he is in a room with a page, and in both he reads the first of the four places and the second and the fourth and does not read the third, and in Chapter 0447 he says one sentence about the top of a page and nobody takes it up.** **The nine inherited debts are carried forward unrepaired and rerouted nowhere**, and are listed in the batch record §6 with the reason for each.

**AND THE MARK THAT CAME BACK OUT OF PLACE, WHICH IS THE ONE THING ON THIS LIST THAT GOT QUIETER.** A page went out of this city on day 438 with four marks on it and came back on day 440 with one of the four in a place it had not gone out in. **Ten chapters went by after that and not one of them says one word about it, and the register's second line records a day and nothing under the day and not one word about a mark, and no chapter of this batch traces a move, investigates one, asks who made one, reports one to a room, guesses at one, or says in any mouth that anybody moved anything. Nobody is going to ask the woman whose mark it is about anything, and the reason is not that she would not answer. A reader who has been given a hunt will read a hunt into a column somebody left empty, and there is no hunt, and this volume does not resolve it and the state layer is not going to.**

**AND THE GAP IN THE CARD FILES IS NOT REPEATED IN A FIFTH SET OF WORDS, because a fifth disclosure is not a remedy and this one has now been made four times in this volume.** The remedy is one outline phase that writes the three missing card files for Volume 09's second, third and fourth batches. **It does not exist, this batch did not write it, and writing one would be an outline's act.** The fault in `outline/volume-09.md` §15's pressure-rotation table — Chapter 0442 in two rows, Chapter 0434 in none, the counts still summing to fifty — is carried forward unrepaired and rerouted nowhere, exactly as Batch 0004 carried it, **and a second fault in the same file is carried with it, the chapter list at §20.6 for the notices and for the three four-place forms naming Chapters 0441 and 0447 where this batch's ten name neither set, published as a departure and not repaired.**

**AND ONE THING A LATER CHAPTER MAY NOT DO, which is inherited and is a rule and not a thread.** A narrator in this volume does not say a chapter number, and does not say that a Bare-Month day is a day of the flood by way of a chapter number. Chapter 0440 did it, it was cut, and **no chapter of these ten does it either, and the ten weekday tokens in this batch's ten leads and bodies name days of the week and Bare-Month ordinals and not one of them maps a day onto a chapter.**

### The next phase

**There is none named here. This volume has no Batch 0006, `outline/volume-09.md` is finished at Chapter 0450, and this batch created no continuation directory, no review prompt, no close prompt, no outline prompt and no Volume 10 directory. The phase that follows this one is chosen by the harness and is not this batch's to name, and a batch that writes its own successor is a batch that has started deciding.**


## Volume 09, Batch 0005 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — and this block wins over every block above it

**Not one item on the live list in this file was discharged, re-opened, re-dated or re-worded by this pass, and a repair pass is the last kind of phase that is allowed to do any of those things by accident. Items moved on pages before this pass and they moved because something was put on a page in Batch 0005, and the prose repair did not put one single new thing on one single page. THIS BLOCK WINS OVER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE IT IN THIS FILE.**

**1. THE EIGHT THREADS BATCH 0005 PUT ON A PAGE ARE ALL STILL OPEN AND ALL STILL SAY WHAT THEY SAID, and the chapters that carry each one still carry it.** The second line in the register, with its day in the first column and nothing under it and no name in the third, and the day on that line being the day the page went out of this city, and both days named in one sentence in one mouth, is still in `chapter-0443.md` and still says *the hundred and twenty-third day of the Bare Month, a Friday, and that is five days counted back from this morning and not counted forward*. The woman's reason for it, said out loud before the pen went near the page, is still in the same chapter in the same mouth. The third question of the woman of about sixty-nine, asked to a room on day 445 and answered by nobody, with about four of the nine having an answer and about four saying nothing, is still in `chapter-0445.md` and is still unanswered. The page waiting for a round and not being folded by anybody who has not been asked is still in `chapter-0447.md`. The man of about thirty-nine being wrong about four stalls and told so in front of about nine people and not told he was right is still in `chapter-0444.md`. The length of new rope going onto the floorboards because a woman moved a chair is still in `chapter-0449.md`, and the man it belongs to still picks it up and puts it back and nobody in that room still says one word about it. The man of about fifty-nine going up a shut road and coming back down it is still in `chapter-0448.md`. The last image of three barrows on a road out of this city is still the last image of `chapter-0450.md`.

**2. THE THING THAT GOT QUIETER IS STILL THE QUIETEST THING ON THIS PAGE, and the repair did not go looking for it.** A page went out on day 438 with four marks on it and came back on day 440 with one of the four in a place it had not gone out in, and ten chapters went by after that saying not one word about it, and the register's second line records a day and nothing under the day and not one word about a mark. **This pass re-read all ten chapters and can now say that every occurrence of the word *mark* in them is inside the word *market*, and that every occurrence of *move* is the light, the chalk, the chair, or the woman's own question about a line. There is no hunt, there was no hunt, and this pass did not supply one.** Nobody is going to ask the woman whose mark it is about anything, and the reason is not that she would not answer. **Open, and not the subject of a sentence in any of the ten, and not going to be the subject of one in Volume 10 either unless a volume that is not this one decides it.**

**3. THE THREADS BATCH 0005 DID NOT TOUCH WERE NOT TOUCHED HERE EITHER.** The fourth of the four said no. The questions of day 346 and day 396. The word on the second slate. The mark in the field. The shut road above this city. The three four-place forms. The keeper of the Redroot gate, the woman of about fifty at a shut gate, the girl of seventeen, the box, the island, the bag, the boards under the chapel, and Break. **A pass that tidies a live list is a pass that has started answering questions nobody asked it, and this one did not touch a single line of the list above this block.**

**4. THE GAP IN THE CARD FILES IS STILL A GAP, AND THIS PASS IS NOT GOING TO CLOSE IT BY WRITING THEM AFTER THE FACT.** `outline/batches/` holds six files and Volume 09 has one, and `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002`, `0003`, `0004` and `0005` were all written from `outline/volume-09.md` alone after their prompts told them to read a card file that was not there. **Batch 0004 disclosed it, Batch 0005 disclosed it for the fourth time and said in writing that a fifth disclosure is not a remedy, and that stands. A card file is an outline file, and a card file written after the ten chapters are on disk is not a card file, it is a reconstruction, and writing one would put a second account of a finished batch into the repository beside the first and leave a writer with two plans and fifty finished chapters.** **So the gap is not closed and is not closable, and the remedy is not a sentence either: the Volume 09 close records it as a debt of the outline phase, and the requirement that a batch's card file exist before its batch runs is written into the close prompt for Volume 10 and into nothing else.** *Open, and it is a debt against the Volume 09 close and not against Batch 0005.*

**5. THE TWO FAULTS IN THE PLAN ARE CLOSED AND ARE NOT THREADS ANY MORE, and closing them is the only state change this pass made to anything but sentence joinery.** `outline/volume-09.md` §15's pressure-rotation table listed Chapter 0442 twice and omitted Chapter 0434 from every row, and the seven counts summed to fifty over forty-nine distinct chapters, and two batches carried it unrepaired. **It is closed: eighteen, thirteen, seven, six, four, one and one, which is fifty entries and fifty distinct chapters and none twice and none missing.** `outline/volume-09.md` §20.6's third column named Chapters 0441 and 0447 where the written chapters name neither the notices nor the three forms, and named three more entries the files do not support. **It is closed: six entries corrected against the files, no count in that table moved.** *Closed, and closed without a plot change and without a card moving.*

### The next phase

**`workspace/volume-09/close/PROMPT.md`, and it is a CLOSE and not a batch.** The volume is finished on day 450, the hundred and thirty-fifth day of the Bare Month, and there is no Batch 0006 and no Chapter 0451. **The five sentences in the block above this one that said this batch created no next phase of any kind are superseded: a close prompt now exists, it is the one the brief asks for, and it names no Volume 10 and writes no chapter.** The close records this volume, carries the six open debts forward, and creates exactly one next phase, which is the Volume 10 outline. It may not write prose and it may not decide the Bare Month's length.

# VOLUME 09 — CLOSE — and this block is at the foot of this file and it wins over every block above it

**A close ran on Volume 09. It did not discharge a thread, did not re-open a thread and did not re-date a thread, and it created exactly one next phase: `workspace/volume-10/outline/PROMPT.md`, a volume outline phase and not a batch. No day moved, no cast member moved, no decision of record was withdrawn, and `outline/ending.md` was opened once and nothing in it moved. This block wins over every block above it, and the full list with its chapters is at `state/volume-09-close.md` §3 and `state/continuity.md`'s newest block.**

## 1. TWELVE THREADS THAT ARE LIVE INTO WHATEVER COMES NEXT, and why each one is on this list

1. **A sheet that has been up a four-hundred-mile road and come back with one of four lines in a place it did not go out in, and nobody in this city is going to be asked about it.** Day 438 out, day 440 back, a Friday and a Sunday. **THE THREAD MOST LIKELY TO BE ACCIDENTALLY RETIRED BY A WRITER WHO WANTS A PLOT, AND THE RULE AGAINST IT IS AT `state/volume-09-close.md` §2 item 1 AND THE PENALTY FOR IT IS A RESOLUTION THIS SERIES HAS REFUSED FOUR VOLUMES RUNNING. A later volume may not trace it unless tracing it is a decision to trace it, and that decision is an outline's to take.**
2. **A register with a third column empty on purpose and a second line in it carrying the day the page went out and nothing under the day.** The line was entered on day 443 and the day on it is day 438 and both days are on the page in one sentence in one mouth. **A blank is the only thing in four hundred miles a return cannot be filled in from, and a clerk nine hundred miles off is going to read a day and nothing under it and is not going to be able to ask this city for the rest, and the person who leaves that column empty is not going to be asked about it either.**
3. **A shelf with a second slate on it with a date on it and nothing under it that nobody has picked up, turned over or written on in fifty chapters.** Named in seventeen of the fifty files and touched in none. **Its measure is unchanged and may not be changed by anybody.**
4. **A question asked three times by one woman in one building about a hundred days apart — a person, a fence, a line — each harder, each got less, and none answered.** She came up nine steps with a tin in both hands on day 445 and the tin was empty and it had been empty on the last two occasions on which she carried it up those steps, and nobody in that room asked her what she carried it for. **A later volume may ask it again and may not answer it.**
5. **A man who speaks the name of this volume once and refuses this volume's one rule ten days later, at his own cost, and is thanked for neither and asked in advance not to be.** Two acts, ten days apart, in the same room, and no chapter of the fifty has him connect them or say he knows what he said.
6. **A man who has been wrong about a market in front of about nine people and would rather have been that than right about a page.** One of the four boards in that market is his. He took a name off his own board in his own hand in about the ninth year of this flood and has never said so to anybody.
7. **A length of new rope that has never been used, and that fell off a chair and was put back by the man it belongs to.** Day 449, a Tuesday. The coil of rope in the handcart on the shut road is a different rope and has been used, and the chapter that names both of them joins them in nothing.
8. **Three barrows on a road nobody in this city can be shown one person on.** They went down on day 391 with a barrow each, a party of four is four, and the count of days out is printed on no page. **A road that can be shown is a road that can be put in a column as empty.**
9. **A woman of about fifty-four whose line is the one that moved and who is never going to be asked about it.** On a page once, in Chapter 0420, put there by being asked in a room in daylight with a date on the door where her line is, with a length of blue thread tied round her wrist. **The one new person this volume put on a page and the one success it had on that axis.**
10. **The fact that nobody in this city knows when the cart comes round again, and that a woman who keeps a public register walked a market on two feet asking four people who sell things and has stopped.** The next round comes on its own day and not on anybody's. **The four days of notice are spent and there is no instrument left in this flood for asking anybody anything in advance of anything, and that is why the man of about fifty-seven is not asked, and it is a consequence and not a decision anybody took about him.**
11. **A man who cannot move a thing and has not tried to move it, and a fence of sixteen posts and eleven withies, and about four feet that is a number in a man's mouth and not a measurement.** Not asked, not told, not defeated, not leaving, not happy. **The fence and whether it works are not settled and are not to be settled by anybody who finds them unsettled.**
12. **The days, which is a figure and not a thing.** The count of this city's silences is derived, on no page, in no mouth, and printed in no chapter: **299 at day 400, 310 at day 411, 319 at day 420, 338 at day 440 and 348 at day 450 on the corrected chain.** `bible/power-system.md` §51 item 9 agrees with that chain and is not in conflict with it: it states the rule and the figure in one clause with one input left open — three hundred and forty-nine less one for every day inside the range on which the wall says something, and Volume 09 has one panel to spend and has not decided its day — and day 438 closes the input at 348. **No amendment is owed and no disagreement is carried.**

## 2. WHAT THIS CLOSE DROPPED FROM THE HEAD OF THE LIST, AND WHY, because a record that silently drops is a record nobody can check

**Dropped from the twelve, not discharged: the Crown's schedule, which stands and is correct and is never opened; the Regent, who is nine hundred miles away; the girl of seventeen, who is on no roll and in no chapter of this volume; the fourth house at Nine Elms; the ninth year of this flood, which no chapter prints; the keeper of the Redroot gate, whose days are not counted; the room at the far end of the Narrow Mark; the boards under the chapel; the island; the box; the man of about thirty-two on the bank; the man of about thirty-seven in `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0293.md`; the consent fracture and the fifth condition, which is not mended and which nobody may mend; the two women of twenty-nine, who have never been in one room and are not in one in this volume; the two rolls of the same gates; the figure the man of about thirty-eight has in his head, which is on no page; the four hundred miles and the page that came from that town; the word on the second slate; the two holes nine feet apart; the fourth line that cannot be administered; the store's board unwritten since day 225; and the barrow of boards against the wall of the shut gate, which does not exist after day 347. Every one of them is live in the state layer and every one of them is at its full length in `state/volume-08-close.md` §2 and §4 and in `outline/volume-09.md` §16. NONE OF THEM IS DISCHARGED BY BEING LEFT OFF THIS PAGE.**

## 3. THE ELEVEN DEBTS, ALL UNPAID, AND WHERE THEY ARE

**All eleven are at `state/volume-09-close.md` §7.7 with their line numbers and their strings, and this close repaired none and took none.** Nine inherited and two found by this phase. **Routed to a review repair pass and not to a writer, and a close phase that quietly takes a routed item to save itself a line is how nineteen quotation marks became a four-volume debt, and this is the fourth volume that has said that sentence.**

## 4. THE ONE THING A WRITER IS MOST LIKELY TO GET WRONG NEXT, AND IT IS NAMED HERE SO THAT IT CANNOT BE

**THE MARK THAT CAME BACK OUT OF PLACE IS NOT A THREAD TO BE PICKED UP. It is a hole in a page and not a mystery, and the volume that made it spent fifty chapters proving that a room cannot be asked about it and that no hunt would have been a hunt. A later volume may not trace it, investigate it, guess at it, report it to a room, or say in any mouth that anybody moved it, UNLESS TRACING IT IS A DECISION TO TRACE IT, and that decision has to be taken in an outline, in its own words, and it has to name what it costs, because the alternative is a hunt with a chain in a book that has refused four hunts in a row.**

## 5. THE NEXT PHASE, BY PATH

**`workspace/volume-10/outline/PROMPT.md`, a volume outline phase and not a batch. It writes `outline/volume-10.md`, Volume 10's first batch's cards at `outline/batches/volume-10-batch-0001.md`, and no prose, and it creates no second phase of any kind.**

## 6. THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS ON THE VOLUME 09 CLOSE, WHICH CLOSED ONE THREAD AND OPENED NONE

**This block wins over every block above it. A repair pass closes a thread and opens none, and the one it closed is item 12, and it closed it because the thread was never a thread.**

**ITEM 12, THE DAYS, AND IT IS CLOSED, AND NOT BECAUSE THE FIGURE MOVED.** The count of this city's silences is derived, on no page, in no mouth, and printed in no chapter: **299 at day 400, 310 at day 411, 319 at day 420, 338 at day 440 and 348 at day 450, and every one of those five figures is unchanged by this pass.** What changed is the second half of the item, which said that `bible/power-system.md` §51 item 9 prints three hundred and forty-nine at day 450 where its own rule gives three hundred and forty-eight, and that §51 declares itself through a repair pass so it stands and the disagreement is published. **There is no disagreement. §51 states the rule and the figure in one clause and leaves one input open on purpose — three hundred and forty-nine less one for every day inside the range on which the wall says something, and Volume 09 has one panel to spend and has not decided its day — and day 438 is that panel, so the input closes at 348 and 348 is what §51's own clause gives.** Six records had carried the other reading, four of them as a standing prohibition on amending §51, and every one of them is withdrawn. §51 is unamended, is whole, and is owed nothing.

**THE TWELVE THREADS ABOVE CARRY FORWARD WHOLE AND AT THEIR LENGTH, and none of them was closed, re-dated, re-worded or softened by this pass: the name of this volume and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; the volume's one rule; the answer of Chapter 0357 and the refusal of Chapter 0393 and the six words of that refusal; the day-154 sentence; the four of the four who said no and the eighteen days nobody said it again; the woman of about sixty-nine and her tin; the woman of about twenty-four and the naming of a commons; the woman of about fifty-four and where her line is; the mark that came back out of place and the ten chapters that passed over it in silence; the four lines drawn and one of them that came back; the third place left empty; and Adrian Vale, who is named in nine chapters of fifty and in furniture in both of Volume 09's last ten, and which is a finding about a rule and not about a chapter, and the rule is an outline's to set and this volume's close did not set it.**

**AND THE ONE POINTER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE GETS WRONG, SO THAT IT IS WRITTEN DOWN ONCE HERE: the panel of Chapter 0393 is at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and not at 1686, and the six words of that refusal also stand twice in this repository's own Volume 08 plan at `outline/volume-08.md:208`, and a plan is not a page.**

NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED. The hold is on this phase's own files and not on where a string stands.**

**NO THREAD WAS CLOSED AND NO THREAD WAS OPENED BY THIS PASS, and the two things a reader is being carried toward are exactly where they were.** The woman's four questions remain three asked and none answered, and the fourth is not asked in this volume and the cost of that is still paid. The figure on the board still goes up on day 490 and stays up and is still not checked. The mark that came back out of place is still not traced and the hole in the book that leaves is still a hole. The name of this volume is still spoken on day 486 and is still on that page and in no file, and this file does not have it and does not say that it is in no file.

**WHAT WAS CLOSED IS A CONTRADICTION AND NOT A QUESTION, and the two are different and only one of them is a thread.** The plan said one thing about Chapter 0453 and five things about it, and the plan said two things about how long that board had stood unwritten, and neither of those was a thread anybody was following. **A batch that finds either of them in the plan will not now find it, and that is the whole of what was closed.**

**AND THE ONE THING A WRITER AFTER THIS ONE SHOULD KNOW, because it is a habit and not a fact about this volume: THE FIGURE OF A SPAN AND THE SPAN ITSELF ARE TWO DIFFERENT THINGS IN THIS REPOSITORY AND THE PHASE THAT WROTE THE PLAN HAD THEM CROSSED ON THE ONE PAGE THAT IS THE HARDEST TO WRITE. A board's silence began on day 225. A man's washing of it began in the seventh week. The distance between those two days was printed on day 451 as if it were the only one of them, and it is not, and the number that made it look like only one of them is a trap that belongs to the last page of the volume.**

## VOLUME 10, BATCH 0001 — the open-threads layer after days 451 to 460, and this block is at the foot and wins over every block above it

**This is a record of pages. Ten chapters of Volume 10 exist and were read for this block. No thread below is closed by this block, none is re-dated, none is re-worded and none is softened, and the one thread this batch moved on is the one it moved on deliberately and says so.**

### 1. The twelve inherited threads, after ten days

1. **A sheet that came back with one of four lines in a place it did not go out in, and nobody in this city is going to be asked about it. STILL OPEN AND NOT TRACED, AND THE COST IS PAID TWICE OVER.** **Ten chapters of Volume 10 now stand on either side of it and not one of the twenty says one word about it.** The mark is not traced, not investigated, not guessed at, not reported to a room, and no chapter of the ten says in any mouth that anybody moved anything on any page, **and the word *moved* stands three times across the ten files and all three are the light coming off flags, a chair that can be moved in a room, and a tin that nobody moved.** The hole in the book is now nineteen chapters wide at the end of this batch and forty-nine chapters wide at the end of the volume. A later volume may trace it only by taking that decision again and naming its cost.
2. **A register with a third column empty on purpose, and two lines in it each carrying a day and nothing under the day. STILL OPEN AND UNTOUCHED IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** **The register was not opened in any of the ten, its third column is empty on purpose on all ten days and its second column is empty on all ten days, and the second slate on the shelf behind it was named in four chapters and in none of them picked up, turned over or written on, and no hand but the register-keeper's went near that shelf.** The third line is entered on day 493 with a day in it and a figure under the day, and the day on that line is day 490, and the two days are three days apart and both are named in the chapter that enters it. **There is a second slate in this batch and it is a different one — under the arm of the woman who walked a market — and nothing is ever written on it either.**
3. **A shelf with a second slate on it that nobody has picked up, turned over or written on. STILL OPEN, AND THIS BATCH ADDED A MEASUREMENT OF IT, AND A REVIEW RE-RAN THAT MEASUREMENT AND HALF OF IT WAS WRONG.** Named in Chapters 0452, 0454, 0455 and 0459, four of the ten, and untouched in all four. **The word-run instrument returns the second-slate frame at twenty-nine words between Chapters 0452 and 0454 of this batch's own ten, which is a frame and not a duplication, and that figure re-ran and stands, and the frame had to be varied in Chapter 0459 on a first pass because the run inside the batch was forty-five words.**

   **AND THE CROSS-CORPUS HALF OF THE SENTENCE ABOVE WAS A POINTER TO A FILE THAT DOES NOT EXIST AND A NUMBER THAT NO FILE CARRIES, and both are corrected here rather than deleted, because a corrected measurement and a deleted measurement leave a later phase with the same wrong figure in its head.** `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0395.md` is not on disk: **Volume 09 is 0401 to 0450 and Chapter 0395 is in Volume 08.** Against the real `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0395.md`, which does carry a second slate, **this frame's longest shared run is EIGHT words, from Chapter 0452.** The twenty-six words belong to two other frames and the figure is theirs, not this one's: **a piece of chalk in a pocket and not taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, at thirty-seven words in Chapter 0454 against `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0411.md`, which is the batch's cross-corpus top and is the chalk and not the slate; and a thing put back where it was by the person whose thing it is, at thirty-three words in Chapter 0456 against `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0449.md`.** All three are frames and none is a duplication, **and a run of forty words or more is what this repository's duplication instrument treats as a duplication, and there is none in these ten on either side of the measure.**
4. **A question asked three times by one woman in one building about a hundred days apart, and none answered. STILL OPEN, AND THE THREAD WENT QUIET ON DAY 459 AND THE COST IS NOW ON A PAGE.** **She came up the nine steps with an empty tin in her two hands and she did not ask a fourth time, and nobody in that room said one word about her not asking, and the reason is an instrument and not a decision about her.** There are **fourteen** days between the day she last asked, which is day 445, and the day she did not ask, which is day 459, and no chapter of the ten says a word about those fourteen days in narration or in a mouth. **The cost is the one the plan named: the series' most legible structural thread went quiet on a page instead of in a state file, and if it is picked up again it will be far more than forty days after the last one.** No chapter of the ten says the three rhyme and no chapter of the ten says the rhyme is the point.
5. **A man who speaks a name once in a room and refuses a rule ten days later, at his own cost, and is thanked for neither. STILL OPEN AND UNTOUCHED IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** He is in five of the ten, as the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, and **nothing in these ten connects his two acts in the volume before this one, mentions either of them except to say that nobody connected them, repeats either speech, or makes the ninth line and the four lines on a sheet the same line.** The ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved.
6. **A man who has been wrong about a market and would rather have been that than right about a page. STILL OPEN, AND HE IS RIGHT AGAIN IN FIVE OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND IS TOLD SO IN NONE.** He is the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board, and he asks the question in Chapter 0452 and is thanked by nobody, and he is right about a page in Chapter 0454 and is thanked by nobody, and he carries the sheet round a market in four hands in Chapter 0460. **He is right in Chapter 0485 about a public figure, at a cost to himself, and is thanked by nobody, and that chapter is not one of these ten.**
7. **A length of new rope that has never been used, and that fell off a chair on day 449 and was put back. STILL OPEN, AND IT FELL A SECOND TIME ON DAY 456 AND WAS PUT BACK AND NOBODY SAID ONE WORD.** **The chapter in which it fell is Chapter 0456 and the room's not-noticing is the whole of that chapter, and the frame is used once in it and not twice.** It is unused on day 460. STILL OPEN, AND UNTOUCHED.** She is not named in any of these ten chapters, she was not asked about anything, and **the descriptor *woman of about fifty-four* is at ZERO across the ten files, which is the correct figure and not a figure about her.** The attribution between `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0294.md` and the woman of Chapter 0420 is still unpaid and is not owed by this batch either.

Chapter 0460 has her holding a page in a market and saying nothing about it.** She is given no age, no descriptor, no number and no name in any of the four, and the descriptor map returns her at zero. **She asks the volume's new question on day 498 and nobody answers it.**

STILL AT ITS FIGURE AND PRINTED IN NONE OF THE TEN CHAPTERS.** The count of this city's silences is derived, on no page, in no mouth, and **is at zero across the ten files of this batch and is computed nowhere in this batch's record**, and the wall said nothing on all ten days of this batch, which is what the volume's plan requires of the other forty-nine.

OPEN AND HALF-WORKED, AND THE WORD IS NOT ON A PAGE.** On day 455 the register-keeper said out loud in a room that nobody in it knows yet whether that figure and this city's own figure are the same kind of thing, and that she is not going to read her own out that morning. On day 457 the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf worked out in about four seconds that the sheet is a count of this city, and he was thanked by nobody and argued with by nobody and the chapter does not say he was right, **and it is not reported to a room, and the reason it is not reported is not a decision anybody took: there is no instrument in this flood to report anything to anybody in advance of anything.** The word itself is worked out on day 463 and is on no page in these ten chapters.

OPEN AND UNTOUCHED IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** He is in four of the ten and **nobody asks him anything in any of them, and the board is washed and put back up in front of about four people in three of them and nobody remarks on it, and on day 458 he hands a rag to a man who has never washed a board and says not one word about that either.** The name is spoken on day 486 and is on that page and in no file this batch wrote.

**Nothing on the lists below was closed, discharged, advanced or resolved by these ten chapters, and the two new things that were opened are named at §1 and §2 and are opened by pages and not by a plan.** Where a thread is named here at a shorter length than the close record gives it, the close record is the inheritance and this block does not restate it at less than its length.

### 1. The two things this batch opened, and the pages they are on

17. **A UNIT, SPOKEN ONCE, IN A WOMAN'S MOUTH, IN A ROOM WITH A DATE ON THE DOOR. OPEN, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THE WORD IS ON A PAGE IN THIS MANUSCRIPT.** In Chapter 0463 the woman of about fifty-two says that the office's figure is a count of places and this city's figure is a count of people and that the whole of the difference between them is a unit, and that a unit is a decision somebody has made about what a thing is, and that nobody in the room was asked about it. **The word stands at two across the ten files of this batch and both are in that one mouth, and it may not be used in narration, it may not be put in a second mouth in this volume, and it may not be in the name the volume speaks on day 486.** Nobody improved on it and nobody thanked her. **OPEN, UNTOUCHED AND CARRIED.**
18. **A COUNT MADE ON FOOT ACROSS ELEVEN MILES OF FLATS, AND THE FINDING THAT IT IS A NUMBER OF PLACES. OPEN, AND IT IS THE FIRST OF THREE MORNINGS, AND THE OTHER TWO ARE NOT NAMED.** In Chapter 0466 a man walks the length of those flats in weather with a slate and a stick, **the slate being the register-keeper's own working slate off the bench in the room over a market, taken without asking and put back on the same bench at the fourth hour, and not the second slate on the shelf behind that bench, which is not picked up and not turned over and not written on**, puts one stroke on the slate for every place there is anybody at, and comes back with about four hundred and forty on it. **The chapter does not say whether that is the figure a man at the bottom of this city sends up a road, and nobody in this city has said out loud whether they are the same.** **Days 496 and 497 are not named in Chapter 0466 and the plan's own volume list and the plan's own escalation entry are reconciled by a reading and not by a decision: the three mornings are the three mornings, the first of them is day 466.** **OPEN AND CARRIED, AND THE COUNT IS DONE AGAIN ON DAY 480 AND AGAIN ON DAYS 496 AND 497, and it comes out differently each time, and every one of the three is entered.**

### 2. The thread that was advanced without being resolved

**THE STORE'S BOARD. STILL UNWRITTEN AFTER TWENTY DAYS OF THIS VOLUME, AND A QUESTION IS NOW ON A TABLE ABOUT IT.** The board has stood unwritten since the two hundred and twenty-fifth day of this flood carrying the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day, and it is named in Chapters 0462, 0464 and 0470 of this batch and washed every morning with the wall above it not washed. **In Chapter 0470 a man asked a room out loud to put a figure on it in chalk in the open, and the register-keeper answered a different question correctly, and nobody answered his, and nobody said out loud that nobody had.** The figure goes on it on day 490 and stays up. **The man who washes it is in two of these ten chapters and is asked nothing in either, and the words he is going to speak on day 486 and the rule he is going to refuse on day 490 are in no chapter of these ten and no chapter of these ten puts those two acts anywhere near each other.**

## VOLUME 10, BATCH 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS, and this block is at the foot of this file and it wins over every block above it

**A reviewer returned six blocking and three moderate findings on Chapters 0461 to 0470. THIS PASS TOOK NO THREAD, CLOSED NO THREAD, REOPENED NO THREAD AND REPAIRED NO DEBT. One thread's record was corrected, one new debt was named, and the plan gained a rule that changes how the next three batches are written. Every thread above this line stands exactly as it stood.**

### 1. THE THREAD WHOSE RECORD WAS CORRECTED, and the correction makes it sharper and not weaker

**THREAD 18, A COUNT MADE ON FOOT ACROSS ELEVEN MILES OF FLATS. STILL OPEN. STILL THE FIRST OF THREE MORNINGS, AND THE OTHER TWO ARE STILL NOT NAMED.** The correction is to the instrument and not to the thread: **the slate the count is on is the register-keeper's own working slate, off the bench in the room over a market, taken without asking and put back on the same bench at the fourth hour, and it is not the second slate on the shelf behind that bench.** A batch that wants to count again takes that slate off that bench. **AND THE THREAD IS NOW SHARPER FOR IT, and this is the part worth carrying: a man counted four hundred and forty places on somebody else's slate and put it back, and the thread's own shape — three mornings, three figures, all three entered, none rubbed out — now has a man who has to go and take the slate off somebody else's bench every time he goes out there. The instrument is the cost and there is no other one in this city.** The second count is on day 480 and the other two are on days 496 and 497, it comes out differently each time, and every one of the three is entered. Days 496 and 497 are named in no chapter of the batch behind you and are not to be named in the batch in front of you.

### 2. THE ONE NEW DEBT, NAMED, UNPAID, AND ROUTED AWAY FROM EVERY WRITER

**A CARD FILE FOR VOLUME 10'S BATCHES 0002, 0003, 0004 AND 0005 DOES NOT EXIST AS AN OUTLINE-LAYER FILE, AND THE ONE THAT DOES EXIST WAS WRITTEN BY A BATCH, WHICH A BATCH IS NOT PERMITTED TO DO.** The collision was published three times before this pass — in the batch record at §0, in `state/current.md` §6, and in the prompt of the batch now in flight, which instructed that next batch to repeat it. **It is routed now and the routing decision is `outline/volume-10.md` §21: a batch authors nothing under `outline/`; `outline/batches/volume-10-batch-0002.md` stays, is registered as batch-authored and pending ratification, and is not ratified; a batch plans in its own record with its ten cards at the head of it before its first chapter; and the debt for the four missing card files is owed by the next OUTLINE phase and by no writer and by no repair pass.** Carried, unpaid, named here so that a later phase does not find it as a surprise and take it as permission.

### 3. THE THIRTY CHAPTERS THIS PASS HAS NOW PUT A FLOOR UNDER, and it is a plan rule and not a thread

**`outline/volume-10.md` §17.18: every batch of ten carries at least one thing that is different at the end of it from the start and names it in one line, and no more than two of a batch's ten chapters may close on a movement that states nothing changed.** Five of the ten chapters of the batch behind you closed on an explicit no-change coda, and the batch's own record conceded that it has no climax but a question with no answer in it, and the reviewer is right that the root cause is the plan. **This opens no thread and closes none; it is a floor under the thirty chapters that remain and it binds from Chapter 0471 forward and not backward.** **§17.19 puts the same floor under the protagonist: in each of his fifteen chapters he must be the cause of at least one thing that happens to somebody else, and the not-asking stays exactly as long as it is.** §17.20 and §17.21 are prose rules and open nothing: paragraph composition published as four counts and never as their sum, and the ten closing paragraphs read side by side as a slot set, which is four lines of work and is the only check in the volume a person does rather than a script.

### 4. THE ELEVEN INHERITED DEBTS AND THE TWELVE ROUTED FIGURES CARRY FORWARD WHOLE AND UNPAID

**None was taken, repaired, rerouted or partially discharged by this pass. The moved mark from day 440 is not traced. The three questions of day 346, 396 and 445 are unanswered and the fourth is unasked. The woman of about fifty-four's unpaid attribution is unpaid. The fourth of the four is not counted. The man of about fifty-seven is not asked and not told and not defeated. The ninth line is unmoved, undated, unread aloud and untaken down. The woman who walked a market has no number and no descriptor and nobody is asking her why she stopped. The box is shut, the island is not owned, the boards under the chapel are not lifted, the girl of seventeen is not fetched, the keeper of the Redroot gate is not used, and the consent fracture is not mended. *Sledge* is at zero in this batch's own files and the inherited list's error is not this pass's to repair. And the volume's one panel, the one name, the one rule and the one day-154 sentence are held and are printed in no file this pass wrote, and this block certifies no absence of any of them.**

## VOLUME 10, BATCH 0003 — the count is done twice now, and there is a hole in the manuscript at day 475

### 1. THE THREAD THAT WAS ADVANCED, AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S SPINE

**THREAD 18, A COUNT MADE ON FOOT ACROSS ELEVEN MILES OF FLATS. STILL OPEN, AND THERE ARE NOW TWO FIGURES ON ONE SLATE AND NEITHER IS RUBBED OUT.** The first was made on day 466 and the second on day 480, **on the same ground, by two different men, with the same mark — one stroke for every place there was a door at — and the only difference between them is that the second man knocked.** About four hundred and forty on the first, about four hundred and thirty on the second. **THE SLATE IS THE REGISTER-KEEPER'S OWN WORKING SLATE IN BOTH CASES, taken off the same bench in the sixth hour without asking and put back on the same bench at the fourth hour, and it is a third object and it is NOT the second slate on the shelf behind that bench, which is not picked up, not turned over and not written on. THE FIRST SET OF MARKS IS STILL ON THE SAME FACE OF IT AND THE SECOND SET WAS MADE DOWN THE OTHER SIDE, so that the two can be looked at together.** **THE THREAD IS SHARPER FOR THE INSTRUMENT THAN IT WAS AND THE COST IS NAMED: a man has to go and take the slate off somebody else's bench every time he goes out there, and it is not his, and nobody asked him to and nobody said anything about it. The register was not opened on either day, the second column held nothing on either day, and the third column is empty on purpose and stays empty on purpose until the line is entered on day 493.** The third count is on days 496 and 497, it comes out differently again, and every one of the three is entered. **Days 496 and 497 are named in no chapter of this batch and are not to be named in the batch after it.**

**AND THE MAN WHO MADE THE SECOND COUNT IS A MAN WITH HANDS AND A REASON THAT IS NOT SENT BY ANYBODY.** He is the man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living, and the chapter says what the hands are for. **And the thread's own shape has a new strand in it: a count of places cannot be turned into a count of people except by knocking at every door and standing there, and there is exactly one man in this city's cast whose trade is standing at other people's doors, and he said the thing that makes him that man out loud in a room on day 477 and nobody in the room acted on it in the chapter.** She — the woman who walked a market on two feet, who has stopped, and who has no age and no descriptor on any page — **is still not on any count and is still at zero across these nine chapters**, and the reason is the volume's own and is at `outline/volume-10.md` §16.9 and is unchanged: she is the only person in this flood who ever did the one thing this manuscript has no instrument for, and a chapter that puts her on a count is a chapter that turns the person who used this manuscript's missing instrument into a document.

### 2. The thread that was advanced without being resolved

**THE STORE'S BOARD. STILL UNWRITTEN AFTER THIRTY DAYS OF THIS VOLUME, AND A FIGURE HAS BEEN DECIDED FOR IT AND IS NOT ON IT.** The board stands unwritten carrying the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day, and it is named in Chapters 0471, 0476 and 0479 of this batch and washed every morning with the wall above it not washed. **In Chapter 0476 a woman with a stall, who has no descriptor and is given none, says out loud in a market what a figure on a wall would be worth to a person who sells off a board, and nobody thanks her and nobody improves on it, and the cost is hers.** The store's own chalk is in a box on the step and the lid is shut in the chapter's last line, **and the man of about thirty-four's own piece of chalk is in his own inside breast pocket in Chapter 0471 and is not taken out, and the two chalks are in two chapters and in two different hands and are never in one.** The figure goes on the board on day 490 and stays up. **The man who washes it is in three of these nine chapters and is asked nothing in any of them, and the words he is going to speak on day 486 and the rule he is going to refuse on day 490 are in no chapter of these nine, and no chapter of these nine puts those two acts anywhere near each other, and no chapter of these nine remarks on the fact that nobody has ever asked him what he is doing to that board, and that omission is the volume's protection for day 486 and is not an oversight.**

### 3. THE THREAD THAT WAS ADVANCED AND IS A MAN WITH AN OFFER HE HAS NOW SAID ONCE

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL HAS SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE WOULD GO AND MAKE THE OTHER NUMBER HIMSELF.** Chapter 0472, day 472, the hundred and fifty-seventh, the room over a market. He said it once, to the room, and he said in the same breath that he is not going to say it a second time in that room, **and nobody in that room accepted it and nobody refused it, and he said nothing more about it for the rest of that morning and nobody in that room brought it up again.** **HE DID NOT GO. Nobody sent him and nobody stopped him, and there is no instrument in four hundred miles in this flood for asking anybody anything in advance of anything, and that is a consequence and not a decision anybody took about him.** **THREAD, OPEN: a man in this city with a stall and a string and a stone has offered himself to walk eleven miles of flats with a slate and make the other number, and the second count on day 480 was made by a different man who was not asked and offered nothing, and the man who offered has a string and a stone and a board that is on a trestle and not on its string in one of these nine chapters and back on it in another. Whether he goes, and when, and with what, is not settled by this batch and is not this batch's to settle.** A later batch may have him go and may not, and a later volume may leave the offer standing indefinitely, and a standing offer is a different thing from a figure on a wall.

### 4. THE THREAD THAT WAS ADVANCED AND IS A QUESTION NOBODY ASKED

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE ASKED A ROOM ON DAY 470 AND GOT NOTHING, AND ON DAY 478 HE STOOD IN THAT ROOM AND ASKED NOTHING.** He came in with his own board under his arm at the eleventh hour on the hundred and sixty-third day of the Bare Month and stood at the back of that room for the rest of the morning. He looked at the man of about fifty-seven and he looked at the length of new rope over the back of that man's chair, and he did not ask him where the fence is, and he did not ask him what the rope was for, and he did not ask him anything at all. **He is right in four seconds in Chapter 0485 and is not thanked and is not argued with and the chapter does not say he was right, and that is the volume's next batch and not this one.** He is not defeated, not converted, does not apologise and does not take his own board down.

FOUR CHAPTERS OF THIS BATCH REFER TO WHAT WAS SAID IN THAT ROOM ON THE SUNDAY AND NONE OF THEM NARRATES IT, AND CHAPTER 0480'S SECOND COUNT IS ON THE GROUND THAT DECISION IS ABOUT.** This is a hole and it is not one any batch may fill, because filling it is deciding something. **3. ## VOLUME 10, BATCH 0004 — the figure is on the board, the name has been spoken, and the hole at day 475 is still a hole

### 1. THE THREAD THAT WAS ADVANCED AND IS THE VOLUME'S SPINE

**THREAD 18, A COUNT MADE ON FOOT ACROSS ELEVEN MILES OF FLATS. STILL OPEN, AND THE THIRD COUNT IS ON DAYS 496 AND 497 AND IS NOT NAMED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH.** Two figures stand on the register-keeper's own working slate, neither rubbed out, the second made on day 480 by the man of about thirty-one who knocks. **THIS BATCH TOOK NO ONE OUT ONTO THOSE FLATS AND NAMED NO FIGURE ABOUT THEM, and that is a decision this batch made about its own ten days and not a resolution of the thread. The thread's next fact is that a figure went onto a public wall on day 490 and stayed there, and the figure on that wall is this city's own and is the one the register is going to carry a line about on day 493.** She — the woman who walked a market on two feet, who has stopped, who has no age and no descriptor on any page — **is in Chapter 0482, in a room, for the whole of a morning, asked nothing, thanked for nothing, given her name to nobody and written down nowhere, and the reason she is in it is a door that would not shut and a man who wedged it open with a piece of the top step.** She is not on a count and she is not in any chapter that is about a figure.

### 2. THE THREAD THAT WAS ADVANCED AND IS NOW THE FIGURE ON A WALL

**THE STORE'S BOARD. WRITTEN ON FOR THE FIRST TIME SINCE THE TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIFTH DAY OF THIS FLOOD, ON DAY 490, IN CHALK, BESIDE THE FIGURES OF THE TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FOURTH DAY, AND NEITHER SET IS RUBBED OUT.** The volume's own figure is on a public wall in chalk and it stays there. **About four people in that market read it that morning and about four of the nine who read it worked out that the two sets do not agree, and none of the nine can say which is right, and the reason none of them can is a word and the word is not on the board.** The wall above the board is not washed and was not washed on day 490 either, and the man who washes the board washes every inch of both sets and stops where the board stops. The store's chalk is in a box on the step and the lid is shut, **and the man of about thirty-four's own piece is in his own inside breast pocket in Chapter 0485 and in Chapter 0490 and is not taken out in either, and the two chalks are in two chapters and in two different hands and are never in one.** THREAD, OPEN: a public wall in a market now carries two sets of figures that disagree, nothing says which is which, and every morning the man who keeps that board clean takes it down and washes both sets and puts it back up.

### 3. THE THREAD THAT WAS ADVANCED AND IS THE VOLUME'S NAME

**A MAN SPOKE THE NAME OF THIS VOLUME ONCE, OUT LOUD, IN THE LOCAL LANGUAGE, IN FOUR TO EIGHT WORDS, IN A ROOM OVER A MARKET IN DAYLIGHT ON DAY 486, IN ANSWER TO ONE QUESTION HE HAD NEVER BEEN ASKED, AND THE WORDS ARE ON THAT PAGE AND IN NO FILE.** Nobody thanked him, nobody improved on it, nobody asked him what he meant, and nobody said it back to him. **FOUR DAYS LATER, ON DAY 490, IN THE SAME MONTH AND A DIFFERENT ROOM, HE REFUSED THIS VOLUME'S ONE RULE IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND WHAT IT COST HIM IS SPECIFIC: the rule told that market the figures he has washed clean every morning since the seventh week of this flood were not checked by anybody either, and that the reason they have stood up is not him and is his not washing the wall above them.** **NO CHAPTER OF THE TEN CONNECTS THE TWO ACTS, no chapter of the ten has him say he knows what he said, no chapter of the ten makes the two sets of figures on that board the same figures, and no chapter of the ten prepares a reader for either.** THREAD, CLOSED AS A THREAD AND OPEN AS A CONSEQUENCE: the name is spoken, the rule is refused, and the two sit four days apart in a book where nobody gets thanked for anything.

### 4. THE THREADS THAT WERE ADVANCED WITHOUT BEING RESOLVED

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR'S OFFER IS SPENT AND HE SAID WHY.** Chapter 0487, day 487: he withdrew it in his own mouth, in a market, in front of nobody he asked, because he cannot knock — a man who sells off a board has a hand that has never been on a door — and a man who went out and did not knock would have come back with a figure and would not have known what he had done, and there is nobody in four hundred miles who could have told him afterwards. **Nobody improved on it, nobody thanked him, and he does not offer himself to anything again in this market.** The thread does not close on him as a defeat: he said a true thing about a true limit and it cost him the only thing he had offered. **THREAD, OPEN: whether he ever walks the eleven miles and what he would take is not settled and is not this volume's to settle.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE HAS AN UNFILLED SPACE ON HIS OWN BOARD AND FOUR PEOPLE HAVE LOOKED AT IT.** Chapter 0485 puts it on a page: he took a name off that board in his own hand in about the ninth year of this flood and has never said so, the space is not filled, and nobody at that end of that market has ever asked him about it. **In the same chapter he is right about a public figure in about four seconds, is thanked by nobody, is argued with by nobody, and is then asked a question for the first time in as long as he has carried a board and has to say he does not know. That is what being right costs, and it is paid in a morning and not in a volume.** THREAD, OPEN: the space is on a page now and nobody has asked him about it and he has not offered it.

**A COUNT OF PLACES WENT DOWN A LANE AND A MAN WOULD NOT WRITE OVER A NAME.** Chapter 0484: eight notches in eight door-frames, a ninth frame with a name cut low into it where a child could reach it, a ninth mark put on the ground instead, and the water took it. **Tamsin Quill said once, in her own mouth, in a lane, and not to be improved on, that a figure that says how many of something there are is the plainest instrument of that claim this flood has produced, and that it is plainer than a notice and plainer than a name, and that the figure is the one that counts and the name is the one that finds people.** THREAD, OPEN: a woman of twenty-nine said it once in a lane to the only man in that lane and he did not answer her, and it is on a page and in no state file and it is not to be improved on.

**A SHED DOOR ON A WHARF WAS REHUNG THE WRONG WAY.** Chapter 0488: it will only shut if you pull it, that shed has not shut since, and a man has to walk round the outside of it every morning to get at boards he wants, and nobody on those boards has said the name of the man who put a plank under that frame. **The tally-board still has about four hundred and forty on it and has not been taken down and put up again.** THREAD, OPEN, and small, and it costs a man a walk every morning.

**THE FENCE HAS NOW BEEN SAID OUT LOUD BY TWO MEN AND THE TWO COUNTS ARE THE SAME.** Chapter 0489: sixteen willow posts and eleven withies, the eleventh withy loose at the top of the ninth post, looked at four times and not bound up again, and nobody in four hundred miles can be shown where any of the sixteen is standing. **THREAD, OPEN, and specifically: whether the fence stands between the right two things is still not settled, and about four feet is still in nobody's mouth, and the man of about fifty-seven was in none of this batch's ten chapters and nobody asked him anything.**

## VOLUME 10, BATCH 0005 — Chapters 0491 to 0500, and this block is at the foot of this file and it wins over every block above it

**A reviewer reads this block for the threads the ten days opened, closed, or left where they were, and not for the measurements, which are in `state/batch-summaries/volume-10-batch-0005.md`.**

### 1. THE BOARD NOW CARRIES THREE SETS OF FIGURES, AND TWO OF THEM ARE THIS CITY'S OWN AND DISAGREE WITH EACH OTHER. THREAD, OPEN.

**The account of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood, a figure of this city's own that went up on the hundred and seventy-fifth day of the Bare Month, and a second figure of this city's own that went up on the hundred and eighty-fifth. NONE OF THE THREE IS RUBBED OUT. The board is washed at the fourth hour and the wall above it is not washed. The two of this city's own do not agree with one another, and nothing on that board says which of the three is the one anybody should be reading, and the woman who chalked the third one up said what it was out loud before she did it and was thanked by nobody and told by nobody that she was right. The third set is inherited from `outline/volume-10.md` §9 item 17 and is not an invention of this batch, and the batch record publishes the disagreement between that line and the object inventory's row at §14 item 1.** THREAD, OPEN: which of the three a person in this market will read as a count of them is not settled and no chapter of these ten settles it, and the man of about thirty-nine has said on a page what a public figure does to the people it is about and has not been thanked for it.

### 2. THE REGISTER'S THIRD LINE, ENTERED, WITH A FIGURE IN THE SECOND COLUMN AND NOTHING IN THE THIRD. THREAD, OPEN.

**The day on the line is the four hundred and ninetieth day of this flood and the day of the entering is the four hundred and ninety-third, both named in one sentence in one mouth on the page, and they are three days apart. The figure under the day is this city's own and is not the office's, and the third column is empty and stays empty. The book was opened once, by the woman who keeps it, and put back by her, and a man at the far end of the bench put his hands out towards the shelf and brought them back and nobody said anything to him about it.** THREAD, OPEN: there is no column in that book for a day and a figure under the day together, and she said so out loud and nobody answered her, and that is a fact about a book and not an answer to anything.

### 3. A QUESTION WAS ASKED TO ONE MAN AND WAS NOT ANSWERED, AND THE ROOM DID NOT HEAR IT. THREAD, OPEN.

**On the hundred and eighty-third day of the Bare Month the woman who walked a market on two feet, and who has stopped, and who has no name and no number and no descriptor on any page, put one question to the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board and to nobody else: what is a word for, in a count, when the only thing anybody will use it for is to make two numbers the same. He did not answer it and the room did not hear it, and the chapter says so in the next line. Nobody thanked her for asking it and nobody told her it was a good question or a bad one. HE PUT HIS THUMB ON THE SPACE ON HIS OWN BOARD WHERE A NAME IS NOT AND TOOK HIS HAND OFF IT AGAIN, and nobody asked him about the space and he has still not offered it, and a man of about thirty-four saw that space in an earlier batch and said out loud that there was a gap in the writing on it.** THREAD, OPEN: the space is on a page, a second man has seen it, a third man has put his thumb on it, and nobody has asked him about it, and the question that was put to him is on a page in a different chapter and nothing in either chapter says the two are connected.

### 4. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR'S BOARD IS FACE IN AND STAYS FACE IN. THREAD, OPEN.

**He took it down off its string on the hundred and eightieth day of the Bare Month and did not put it up again, and the face of it went into the back of his own trestle. The string and the stone are on the post with nothing at the end of the string, and a man who has gone down that row for nine years came to the end of it, looked at a post and a bare back of a board, and went on without stopping, and it is not a thing that man does. On the day before that, a hand that is nobody's lifted the stone at the foot of his string out of the water and drew the string up a hand's width and put a length of split wood under the near corner of his board, and that man has spent a day reaching for the foot of his own board with one hand and got a wet band on his sleeve, and he has not said who did it and the woman two stalls along has not said who did it either.** THREAD, OPEN: the board is face in, the offer from that stall is closed, his piece of chalk is still in his own inside breast pocket and did not come out on either day, and nobody has asked him about any of it.

### 5. TWO FIGURES OF THE SAME KIND OF THING ON ONE FACE OF ONE SLATE, AND A PLACE WHERE A THING IS A DOOR ON ONE SIDE AND A WALL ON THE OTHER. THREAD, OPEN.

**The third count made on foot across eleven miles of flats came out different again, and the reason is neither the weather nor a knock, and the reason is a place about two thirds of the way along a line of doorways where a man on the road side says door and the same man on the channel side says wall, and both go into the book as the same thing, and a barrow goes on the road side. NEITHER FIGURE IS PRINTED ANYWHERE IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS and the two of them are on one face of the register-keeper's own working slate with neither rubbed out, and the slate came back into this city face up on a barrow under a fold of sacking that was not taken off again.** THREAD, OPEN: nobody has walked that line from the far side, and the man who did walk it is the man who carried the slate and he is not going to go back and look for the place again to see whether he has it right, and that is on a page.

### 6. A SHEET OF PAPER WITH A FIGURE ON IT IS FACE DOWN ON A TABLE IN A ROOM. THREAD, OPEN.

**A man copied the figure off that wall in his own hand, with a piece of chalk and a slate and nothing else, and carried it up a stair, and put it face down on a table in the room over a market, and stood at the window with his back to it for the rest of that morning. Nobody in that room turned it over, about four of the nine knew what was on it, and about four had come up that stair without knowing. The woman who keeps the register did not ask him for it and did not ask him to take it away. IT HAS NO HEADING ON IT AND NO PLACES RULED UNDER ONE AND IS NOT A DOCUMENT OF THIS FLOOD, and no hand in that room but the one that put it there is ever going to be the hand that turns it.** THREAD, OPEN: it is the only copy of that figure in this city and it is face down, and the man who made it is unnamed and is not the man of about thirty-nine.

### 7. THE SHED DOOR ON THE SALT WHARF. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS SMALLER THAN THE OTHERS.

**It went back on the wrong side of its opening on the hundred and seventy-third day of the Bare Month and did not shut again until the hundred and seventy-ninth, when a man pulled it to and it stood shut for about as long as it takes a man to count nine, and the wind came through the gap at the top of it and it stood open again. A stack came down on the end boards that morning instead of on the run of boards that man had been walking round a building every morning to get at, and nobody on those boards has said the name of the man who put a batten under the foot of it. The tally-board still has its figure on it and that figure is not written down anywhere in this flood.** THREAD, OPEN: the door still does not shut, and nobody has been shown the whole of the thing.

### 8. THE FENCE, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN, AND THE LENGTH OF NEW ROPE. THREAD, OPEN AND UNTOUCHED.

**A fence is named once in these ten chapters, standing along the top of the shut road in the last one, with no post and no withy and no total of any kind, and nobody measures anything. The man of about fifty-seven is in NONE of these ten chapters and the length of new rope is named in NONE of them, and the word *rope* is at zero across all ten. THE PLAN PUTS CHAPTER 0494 IN THE ROPE ROW AND THIS BATCH DEPARTED FROM THAT ROW AND PUBLISHED THE DEPARTURE, because the man the rope belongs to is in a room over a market and not in a market or on a wharf, and this batch did not go and find him. Nobody asks him where the fence is, nobody tells him, and nobody defeats him, and about four feet is not printed in any of the ten.** THREAD, OPEN, exactly as it stood.

### 9. THE THREE ON THE ROAD. THREAD, OPEN, AND ONE FIGURE PRINTED.

**Three barrows are on a four-hundred-mile road out of this city, a hundred and nine days out, measured from the day they went, which is the three hundred and ninety-first day of this flood, and that is the only figure of days out that any of these ten chapters prints, and it names its anchor in the same sentence. There is not one person on that road anybody in this city can be shown, and there never has been, and the three of them are on it. A party of four is four and no chapter of these ten counts the days since anybody said no.** THREAD, OPEN: nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road, and a road that can be shown is a road that can be put in a column as empty.

## THE VOLUME 11 OUTLINE, the authoritative block, and it wins over everything above it

**Volume 11 is PLANNED and is on no disk as prose. `outline/volume-11.md` is the plan, `outline/batches/volume-11-batch-0001.md` is the first batch's cards, and the next phase is `workspace/volume-11/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, a BATCH, which writes Chapters 0501 to 0510 and nothing else.
