### 1. THE BOARD WITH THREE SETS OF FIGURES, AND THE QUESTION NOBODY CAN ANSWER. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS THE SPINE OF THE NEXT FIFTY DAYS.

**A board on the outside wall of a store at one end of that market carries the account of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood on the left, a figure of this city's own in the middle put up in chalk on day 490, and a second figure of this city's own on the right put up on day 500 which does not agree with the one in the middle. None of the three has been rubbed out. All three are washed at the fourth hour every morning. The wall above the board is not washed on any day and is not going to be washed in Volume 11, and the man who decided not to wash it is in the volume and is not asked about it. About nine people were at that end of the market when the third figure went up, about four of them read it, and NOT ONE OF THE NINE CAN SAY WHICH OF THE THREE IS THE ONE ANYBODY SHOULD BE READING, and the reason none of them can is a word and the word is not on the board. THREAD, OPEN, and it is not a mystery and it is not a hunt, and no chapter of Volume 11 may resolve it.**

### 2. THE SECOND ONE-PLACE FORM, AND IT IS GOING OUT OF THIS CITY. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS THE ONLY NEW THREAD IN THE VOLUME.

**A sheet with a heading and ONE place ruled under the heading, and the one place is EMPTY, came up the road on day 504 in a satchel with a flap on the quarterly round, out of the same office nine hundred miles off that the return and the last two sheets came from. It is read out on day 506 in the room over the market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, about nine people, and the reader says out loud what it is for before anybody asks. It is not the return, it is not a copy of anything, and IT IS NOT THE FIRST ONE-PLACE FORM COMING BACK, and the two forms are never laid beside each other and never compared out loud. The one place is filled on day 538 with a figure of this city and with nothing else, the form is closed on day 540, and IT GOES OUT OF THIS CITY ON DAY 545. There is no place on it for the person who made the figure. THREAD, OPEN: it is the first figure of this city that has ever left this city on a form, it is not withdrawn, it is not coming back, and nothing in this flood has an instrument for bringing a figure back.**

### 3. FOUR COUNTS MADE ON FOOT, AND THE COLUMN THEY STAND IN. THREAD, OPEN, AND NONE OF THE FOUR AGREES WITH ANY OTHER.

**A person with a slate and a tin counted one kind of thing on foot across eleven miles of flats on four mornings — days 511, 515, 517 and 521 — and the four figures do not agree, and all four are entered. They stand in the second column of this city's public register, one under another, in the order they were made, none struck, above one figure entered on day 493 with a day in it and the day it was written down on. THE THIRD COLUMN IS EMPTY ON ALL FIFTY DAYS AND THE PERSON WHO LEAVES IT EMPTY IS NOT ASKED ABOUT IT. THE TOP ONE OF THE FIVE IS THE ONE THAT WENT OUT OF THIS CITY AND THE BOTTOM ONE OF THE FIVE IS THE FIGURE THAT IS ON THE WALL, and the second slate is on the shelf behind that register with a date on it and nothing under it and is not picked up, not turned over and not written on. THREAD, OPEN, and the volume's reading is that a book reads down where a wall reads across and no instrument in four hundred miles can put a book and a wall in the same order.**

**AND THE DAYS IN THAT SENTENCE WERE 511, 515, 521 AND 528 IN THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS THREAD AND A REVIEW REPAIR PASS REPLACED 528 WITH 517, so that the fourth morning is 521 and the four run 511, 515, 517, 521, because days 522, 525 and 532 of `outline/volume-11.md` each need all four figures to exist and two of the three stood on three figures. Day 528 is not a morning of the count and is not a fifth morning; the whole of the finding, with the first edition of every cell it touched, is at `outline/volume-11.md` §14.5.**

### 4. THE MAN WHO WALKS THE FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD, AND THE QUESTION HE IS NOT ANSWERING. THREAD, OPEN.

**On day 548, in the room over a market, in daylight, with a date on the door, the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register asks one man a question to one man and not to the room: what is a word for, in a count, when the only thing anybody will use it for is to make two numbers the same. The man is the man of about fifty-nine who has walked that road four times in nine years, who has not gone after the three who went down it and is not asking about them. HE DOES NOT ANSWER IT AND THE ROOM DID NOT HEAR IT. THREAD, OPEN: the same question was asked once on day 498 to a different man and was not answered, and a later volume may ask it again and may not answer it.**

### 5. THE THING THE VOLUME SPENT, AND WHOSE IT IS. SPENT, NOT OPEN.

**A figure's ability to stay in this city, spent on day 545 when a form went out of it. The cost was paid by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, who named it out loud first on day 525, and by the woman who walked a market on two feet, who made the figure, is on no roll, has no name and no age and no descriptor on any page in this manuscript, is thanked by nobody, is not asked anything, and cannot be shown to anybody in four hundred miles. NOT OPEN AND NOT REVERSIBLE, and the man who washes the board is in the volume and is not asked about any of it.**

### 6. WHAT CARRIES UNCHANGED AND WHAT DID NOT MOVE

**The eleven inherited debts carry whole and unpaid, named in `outline/volume-11.md` §21.2. `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md` is still not on disk and is owed to a Volume 11 close and to no writer and to no repair pass; this outline named it and neither wrote it nor routed it. The one of four marks that came back out of place on the sheet of day 440 is STILL NOT TRACED and this outline declined to trace it a second time and the cost is the same hole at twice the length. The fence, and whether it stands between the right two things, is not settled. The man of about fifty-seven is not asked about the rope or anything else. The three on the road are at one hundred and ten days out at day 501 and one hundred and fifty-nine at day 550, measured from day 391, and nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road. The seventh form of the calendar does not exist and was not made and was not guessed at, and the Bare Month has no length and no end and was given nothing.**

**AND THE THREE FIGURES THAT WERE WRONG IN A LIVE PLAN ARE NOW PAID, IN ONE CELL EACH, WITH THEIR FIRST EDITIONS PRESERVED BENEATH THE CORRECTIONS: the published zero for the word the volume before this one turned on, at `outline/volume-10.md` §19.3, and the withdrawn silence-count derivation at `outline/volume-10.md` §14.5. NOTHING ELSE IN THAT FILE WAS TOUCHED. The declared-motif list at §20.3 is SUPERSEDED by `outline/volume-11.md` §6.10 and not amended. THE CARD-FILE DEBT IS CARRIED AND NOT PAID, and the reason is published at §21.1 item 2, and the arrangement that stops it growing is published at §21.4.**

### 7. The one thing no file the Volume 11 outline wrote may print

**The name of the volume before the last, and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; that volume's one rule, the panel of day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words are also reproduced at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; the day-154 sentence; the count of chapters carrying the day-154 beat, which the Volume 09 plan withdrew; AND THE WORDS THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN SPEAKS IN CHAPTER 0486. NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED, AND THE MEASUREMENT OF THE HOLD WAS NOT RE-RUN BY THIS PHASE BECAUSE RE-RUNNING IT REQUIRES PRINTING THE SPANS. NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 11 CONNECTS HIS TWO ACTS, ASKS HIM ANYTHING, OR GIVES HIM A LINE TO SAY.**

## VOLUME 11, BATCH 0001 — the state of the live list at day 510, and nothing on it moved

**This block wins over every block above it. The batch that wrote it is `workspace/volume-11/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` and its record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-11-batch-0001.md`. Where this file and a chapter disagree, the chapter is right.**

**1. THE STORE'S BOARD. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS A DAY OLDER.** It carries three sets of figures: the account of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood on the left, a figure of this city's own in chalk in the middle from day 490, and a second figure of this city's own on the right from day 500 which does not agree with the one in the middle. **On the hundred and eighty-sixth day of the Bare Month it was washed twice, and the second pass was a thing a man did on purpose, and none of the three was rubbed out and the wall above it was not washed.** Not one of the nine people who can read it can say which of the three is the one anybody should be reading and the reason none of them can is a word and the word is not on the board. **NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH RESOLVED IT AND NO CHAPTER MAY.**

**2. THE FIRST ONE-PLACE FORM, AND WHERE A FORM GOES. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS THE SMALLEST THING ON THIS LIST.** A sheet with a heading and one place ruled under the heading and a figure in that one place, read out in that room, never returned and never withdrawn. **ON THE HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-EIGHTH DAY OF THE BARE MONTH IT WAS FOUND IN THE TOP DRAWER OF A CHEST UNDER THE SHELF BEHIND THE BENCH IN THAT ROOM, IN A ROOM IN THIS CITY, AND PUT ON THE TABLE AND LOOKED AT AND PUT BACK, AND IT IS NOW ON THE SHELF ABOVE WHERE THE REGISTER STANDS AT THE HEIGHT OF A PERSON'S SHOULDER, AND FROM THE BOTTOM OF THE STAIR IT IS THE FIRST THING IN THAT ROOM ANYBODY CAN SEE.** Nobody in this flood has ever said where a form goes after a person has read it out, and nobody in these ten chapters supplied an answer and nobody was asked for one.

**3. THE SECOND ONE-PLACE FORM, AND ITS ONE EMPTY PLACE. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS THE ONLY NEW THREAD IN THE VOLUME.** A satchel with a flap came up the road on the quarterly round on the hundred and eighty-ninth day of the Bare Month with a sheet in it: a heading at the top, ONE place ruled under that heading, and THE ONE PLACE IS EMPTY. **IT WAS READ OUT ON THE HUNDRED AND NINETY-FIRST BY THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER, IN THE LOCAL LANGUAGE, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE IN CHALK ON THE DOOR AND ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN THE ROOM, AND SHE SAID OUT LOUD WHAT IT WAS FOR BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED: ONE FIGURE OF THIS CITY, IN THE ONE PLACE UNDER THAT HEADING, AND IT IS TO BE SENT BACK. THE NAME OF THE THING THE FIGURE IS ABOUT IS NOT ON THE PAGE AND IS NOT IN ANY MOUTH.** Nobody was sent for anything and no eighth notice went out and the four days of cooling are not available. The one place is filled on day 538 with a figure and with nothing else. **The two one-place forms were in the same room on two of the ten days and were not laid beside each other and were not compared out loud on either.**

**4. FOUR COUNTS MADE ON FOOT. THREAD, OPEN, AND NOT ONE OF THE FOUR EXISTS YET.** A person with a slate and a tin will count one kind of thing on foot across eleven miles of flats on four mornings, **days 511, 515, 517 and 521, AND THERE IS NO FIFTH AND DAY 528 IS NOT A MORNING OF THE COUNT.** **A LONG COUNT MADE ON FOOT WAS COMPARED IN A ROOM ON THE HUNDRED AND NINETY-THIRD AND DID NOT AGREE WITH A COUNT TAKEN OFF THE WALL, AND ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE IN THAT ROOM COULD SAY WHY AND ABOUT FOUR COULD NOT, AND THE REASON IS NOT PRINTED IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** The register's second column holds one figure and the third column is empty on purpose and nothing went into it in these ten days.

**5. THE SECOND SLATE THAT WALKS. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS NEW ON THIS LIST.** A slate on the shelf behind the register, with a date scratched across the head of it and nothing whatever under that day. **THE SHELF IS ON TWO BRACKETS THAT ARE NOT THE SAME HEIGHT AT THE FRONT AS AT THE BACK, AND THE SLATE WALKS DOWNHILL ON IT ABOUT THE WIDTH OF A FINGER EVERY MORNING AND EVERY TIME A HAND GOES NEAR IT, AND A MAN ON THE FLOOR OF THAT ROOM ON THE HUNDRED AND NINETY-FOURTH SAW IT FROM BELOW AND WANTED SOMETHING FLAT TO GO UNDER IT AND PUT NOTHING UNDER IT.** It has not been picked up, turned over or written on in any of these ten chapters and no hand but the register-keeper's went near the things on that shelf.

**6. THE MAN WHO WASHES THE BOARD. THREAD, OPEN, AND HE IS NOT ASKED.** A bucket, a rag, and a wall he has decided not to wash. **He is in three of the ten days and nobody asks him anything in any of them, and on the hundred and ninety-second he turned that trough upside down on the flags and washed the grit out of the bottom of it, which has never been done before in this flood, and the wall above that board is still not washed.** He is not asked why he does not wash the wall and no chapter of this volume may ask him.

**7. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR. THREAD, OPEN.** His board stands face in against his own trestle and the bare back of it is out in the row, and his piece of chalk is in his inside breast pocket and the full form of the day it went in is printed at full length in the two chapters of this batch that carry it. **On the hundred and eighty-ninth he said out loud, once, in about nine words, that the one place on a form is the shape of the second column in the register-keeper's book, and nobody thanked him and nobody said whether it was so.** He has not traded off the face of that board since he took it down and he said in about nine words on the hundred and ninetieth what is wrong with a page and has asked in advance not to be thanked.

**8. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE. THREAD, OPEN.** A board under his arm, nineteen rates, and a space about two fingers wide where a name was in his own hand about nine years ago and was never filled. **On the hundred and eighty-seventh he told a room of about four people that he took it off himself and did not say what it was, and nobody asked him, and on the hundred and ninety-second he went out of that market with the space in it and not one person at either end of that row used his name.** The name is not printed and is not going to be printed.

**9. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE. THREAD, OPEN.** A satchel with a flap. He carried the second one-place form up the road on the hundred and eighty-nineteenth and did not open it, said four words about it, and was asked nothing. **He has carried everything that has come up that road in this flood and has opened none of it, and in three of these ten chapters he sat in a room with the satchel between his knees and nobody asked him what was in it.** The cost of the refusal that is coming on day 540 is his own silence and it is not spent.

**10. THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN. THREAD, OPEN AND UNTOUCHED.** A length of new rope over the back of his chair, unused on day 450, unused on day 500, unused on day 510. **He was in a room on the hundred and ninety-third with the rope over his chair and nobody lifted it and nobody asked him anything and nobody said one word about it.**

**11. THE THREE ON A FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD. THREAD, OPEN.** They went down it on day 391 with a barrow each, **a hundred and seventeen days out at day 508, measured from the day they went, which is the three hundred and ninety-first day of this flood.** Nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road and no chapter of these ten went to it or asked about anybody on it. A party of four set out and three went and one said no before anybody asked her, and no person in any of these ten chapters counted anything about that.

**12. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT MOVE.** The eleven inherited debts carry whole and unpaid. `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md` is still not on disk and is owed to a Volume 11 close and to no writer. The one of four marks that came back out of place on the sheet of day 440 is STILL NOT TRACED. The fence, and whether it stands between the right two things, is not settled and was not measured. The seventh form of the calendar does not exist and was not made and the Bare Month has no length and no end and was given nothing by these ten days.

**13. THE ONE THING NO FILE THIS BATCH WROTE MAY PRINT.** The name of the volume before the last, and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; that volume's one rule, the panel of day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words are also reproduced at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; the day-154 sentence; the count of chapters carrying the day-154 beat, which the Volume 09 plan withdrew; AND THE WORDS THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN SPEAKS IN CHAPTER 0486. NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED, AND THE MEASUREMENT OF THE HOLD WAS NOT RE-RUN BY THIS BATCH BECAUSE RE-RUNNING IT REQUIRES PRINTING THE SPANS. NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH CONNECTS HIS TWO ACTS, ASKS HIM ANYTHING, OR GIVES HIM A LINE TO SAY.

# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0001 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS

**14. THE TWO FIGURES THIS BATCH PUBLISHED ABOUT ITSELF AND GOT WRONG, THREAD, NOW CORRECTED.** The record's paragraph total was 238 and is 180, and a paragraph here is a block between blank lines with the heading line and the `---` rules not counted; the other three readings are 190, 225 and 235 and 238 reproduced on none of them. The record's free-standing one-sentence narration column was 57 and is 13, and the old column counted the whole one-sentence narration column twice under two headings. **BOTH WERE ERRORS IN THE RECORD AND NOT IN A CHAPTER, AND NEITHER IS A DEBT, AND NEITHER IS OWED ANYBODY: they are published and corrected in place, and no next batch inherits them.**

**15. THE SEVEN ROWS OF §1.11 THAT WERE WRONG, THREAD, NOW CORRECTED.** `the nine people` 3→2, `nine people` 12→11, `about nine people` file count 4→6, the case-insensitive count every other row in that column uses, `four hundred miles` 11→10 with Chapter 0501 missing from its own row, `one place` 40→44 with Chapter 0508 missing from its own row, `nineteen rates` 2→4 because a file count stood in the occurrence column, and `sixteen willow posts` 1→0 case-sensitive because the string stands once and stands at the head of a marked turn. **THE RULE §17.12 BINDS IS ABOUT A CHAPTER USING A RECURRING NUMBER WHERE IT IS NOT THE THING THE CHAPTER IS ABOUT, AND NOT ONE OF THESE SEVEN CORRECTIONS CHANGES THAT JUDGEMENT FOR ANY ROW. WHAT MOVED IS THE ARITHMETIC. A PUBLISHED HEAD TABLE THAT CANNOT COUNT IS A STANDING RISK IN THIS REPOSITORY AND THE THIRD REPAIR PASS IN FOUR VOLUMES HAS NOW FOUND ONE.**

**16. THE ONE SENTENCE THAT WAS FALSE ABOUT A DOCUMENT RATHER THAN WRONG ABOUT A NUMBER, THREAD, CLOSED BY CORRECTION.** The record claimed the batch's own brief marked Chapter 0509 as carrying the protagonist. The brief's table leaves that cell blank. What carries 0509 is `outline/volume-11.md` §14.3's Adrian column and §4.1's table of the eleven wants, and a card may take a want from §4.1 and may not supply one. **THE THREE CHAPTERS STAY AND THE DISAGREEMENT WITH THE BRIEF IS STILL PUBLISHED RATHER THAN RESOLVED — IT IS THE PLAN AGAINST THE BRIEF NOW, NOT THE PLAN, THE CARD AND A TABLE THAT ALL AGREED AGAINST THE BRIEF, AND THAT IS A WEAKER DISAGREEMENT AND A MORE HONEST ONE.**

**17. WHAT THE REPAIR DID NOT UNDO.** The volume's one unwashed wall is still unwashed and the three sets of figures are still on the board, in Chapter 0507 and in Chapter 0501. The second slate is still on its shelf with a day across the head of it and nothing under that day. The volume's one panel is still fixed to day 540 and is still unspent, and the wall said nothing on all ten days. The eleven inherited debts still carry whole and unpaid, and `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md` is still not on disk and is still owed to a Volume 11 close and to no writer.

# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0002 — Chapters 0511 to 0520, the authoritative block, and it wins over everything above it

**1. THE COUNT MADE ON FOOT. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT HAS TWO OF ITS FOUR MORNINGS BEHIND IT.** Eleven miles of flats, a channel of wet silt, a line of doorways, and one kind of thing counted: the shut doorways. **DAY 511 IS THE FIRST MORNING AND A FIGURE CAME OUT OF IT; DAY 515 IS THE SECOND MORNING AND A FIGURE CAME OUT OF IT AND IT DOES NOT AGREE WITH THE FIRST. THE TWO STAND ONE UNDER THE OTHER ON THE FACE OF A WORKING SLATE IN THE ROOM OVER THE MARKET WITH ABOUT THE WIDTH OF TWO FINGERS BETWEEN THEM AND THE DAY BESIDE EACH OF THEM IN THE SAME HAND, AND NEITHER HAS BEEN READ OUT AND NEITHER HAS BEEN SENT ANYWHERE.** Days 512, 514 and 519 were mornings on the same flats and no figure came out of any of them. **THE FOUR MORNINGS ARE 511, 515, 517 AND 521, AND THERE IS NO FIFTH AND DAY 528 IS NOT A MORNING OF THE COUNT.** A figure is not in the register's middle column and the register came off its shelf in these ten days and nothing went into it.

**2. THE FIGURE THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT SENDS. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS ON A BOARD IN THE OPEN AIR.** About four hundred and forty, of the same kind of thing as the count on foot, and the figure he has is on no page and is printed nowhere in this volume. **ON DAY 519 HE LAID THE TALLY-BOARD FACE DOWN ON A STACK OF BOARDS AT THE NEAR END OF THE FLATS, WITH THE FIGURE ON THE SIDE THE SUN DOES NOT GET, AND TOOK HIS BARROW UP THE ROW, AND SAID THAT ANYBODY WHO WANTS TO FIND IT CAN COME AND TURN IT OVER AND THAT NOBODY IN THIS CITY IS GOING TO AND NEITHER IS HE.** It is lying there. Nobody in this city has picked it up. The second one-place form, which came up the same road in that satchel on the quarterly round and is on a table in that room with one place under its heading and nothing whatever in that one place, is on the same road and is a fifth document and is not that board and is not compared with anything.

**3. THE WALL ABOVE THAT BOARD. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT HAS BEEN TOUCHED ONCE AND WAS NOT WASHED.** It is not washed on any of these ten days and is not going to be washed on any of the fifty. **ON DAY 516 THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THAT BOARD PUT HIS HAND FLAT ON IT AT THE NINTH HOUR, HELD IT THERE WHILE THE LIGHT CAME OFF THE FLAGS, AND TOOK IT OFF AGAIN WITHOUT A RAG AND WITHOUT WATER.** At about the seventh hour that day he said out loud, to the trough and not to the row, once, that he has not washed it and is not going to and is not going to say why. Nobody asked him why, nobody improved on it, nobody argued with it. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall said nothing about that wall, and the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board stood with his back to it.

**4. THE TROUGH. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS WHERE IT WAS.** It stood under the store's board from the seventh week of this flood until the second hour of day 512, when two people carried it four rows down that row and set it down at the far end of it, and the man who washes that board watched them do it and said nothing. It was back under that board at the seventh hour of day 514, carried there by the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, who was not asked to and was not thanked. **THE RAG WENT INTO IT AT THE NINTH HOUR OF DAY 516 AND DID NOT COME OUT.** One trough, one bucket, one rag.

**5. THE FENCE. THREAD, OPEN, AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN DAYS TOUCHED IT.** Sixteen willow posts and eleven withies, said out loud on day 513 by the man of about sixty to nobody who asked him for them, and the count was not a measurement anybody took. **A CARRIER CAME UP THE SHUT ROAD OUT PAST THE LAST NAMED HOUSE ON DAY 513 WITH AN EMPTY SATCHEL, STOOD AT THE TOP OF IT AT THE NINTH HOUR, DID NOT GO THROUGH, AND WENT BACK DOWN IT, AND NOBODY IN THIS CITY HAS SAID WHERE THAT ROAD IS SHUT OR WHO SHUT IT.** The gap in that fence was not measured and the corner in it was not measured and no post was moved and no withy was bound.

**6. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING. THREAD, OPEN.** A satchel with a flap, the strap round it twice, and nothing in it. He went up the shut road on the quarterly round and did not go through it and went back down it, and he said out loud that he would rather somebody wrote down that he stood there than that they wrote down that he went through. **The cost of the refusal that is coming on day 540 is his own silence and it is not spent and it was not spent on day 513.**

**7. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL. THREAD, OPEN.** His board stands face in against his own trestle with the bare back of it out in the row, and his own piece of chalk is in his own inside breast pocket and the full form of the day it went in is printed at full length in the one chapter of this batch that carries it. **ON DAY 514 HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT THE CHALK IN THE BOX ON THE STEP IS THE STORE'S CHALK AND IS NOT HIS AND THAT HIS OWN HAS BEEN IN THAT POCKET SINCE THE TWO HUNDRED AND NINETY-EIGHTH DAY OF THIS FLOOD, WHICH IS THE FORTY-THIRD DAY OF THE SHORT MONTH; HE DID NOT SHORTEN IT AND HE DID NOT TAKE THE OTHER ONE. HE ALSO SAID THAT THERE IS ABOUT FOUR FEET OF STANDING WATER AT THE NEAR END OF THAT ROW AND THAT NOBODY HAS TAKEN A CORD TO IT AND NOBODY EVER WILL, AND HE CARRIED THE TROUGH BACK THE LENGTH OF THAT ROW AT THE SEVENTH HOUR AND NOBODY ASKED HIM WHY.** On day 519 he walked past that box four times and did not stop at it and did not put his hand over it.

**8. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD. THREAD, OPEN.** A board under his arm, nineteen rates, and a space about the width of two fingers where a name was in his own hand about nine years ago and was never filled. **ON DAY 518 HE PUT THAT BOARD FACE UP ON A TABLE IN THAT ROOM AND WAS RIGHT IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS ABOUT THE ORDER THINGS ON IT STAND IN, AND NOBODY IN THAT ROOM SAID HE WAS RIGHT.** The name is not printed in these ten chapters and nobody in that room asked him about it.

**9. THE WOMAN WHO WALKED A MARKET. THREAD, OPEN, AND SHE HAS NO NAME, NO AGE, NO NUMBER AND NO DESCRIPTOR ON ANY PAGE.** She is in seven of these ten chapters and no descriptor was given her and nobody asked her anything in any of them. **ON DAY 519 A MORNING WAS SPENT ON ELEVEN MILES OF FLATS WITH A SLATE UNDER HER ARM AND A TIN IN HER TWO HANDS AND NO FIGURE CAME OUT OF IT, AND NOBODY IN THIS CITY ASKED HER WHY AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE THE FIRST.** On day 517 she went over two thirds of a channel on a plank that somebody had laid there and came back over it and put the near end of it back with her foot. **THE FIGURE SHE MAKES IS GOING OUT OF THIS CITY WITHOUT HER ON IT AND THERE IS NO PLACE ON ANYTHING THIS CITY HAS WRITTEN FOR A PERSON OF HERS TO BE PUT.**

**10. THE PLANK. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS A NEW OBJECT ON A PAGE IN THIS VOLUME.** A plank lying across about two thirds of the channel between the first row of the flats and the second, put there on day 517 by Adrian Vale, who was chest-deep in that channel for most of the morning, who did not get it to the wet sill of the second row, who was no further forward than the morning before, and who left it where it was. **The man of about thirty-eight got his barrow across the two thirds of it on the wheel and did not look at it. It is not a route and the chapter says so in its own words.**

**11. THE OLD BOOK IN THAT ROOM. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS A NEW FIGURE ON A PAGE.** A figure on the left-hand page belonging to the seventeenth day of this flood, standing on that page through five hundred and two days without a pen near it. On day 519 it was put open on the table in that room beside the working slate and not laid against it, and the three figures on that table do not agree with one another, and nobody in that room said a word about it.

**12. THE REGISTER'S MIDDLE COLUMN. THREAD, OPEN.** Five lines' worth of room, measured with a finger out loud on day 520, and nothing entered in it on any of these ten days, and the column on the right empty from the top of the page to the bottom. **THE SHEET WITH THE ONE EMPTY PLACE WAS TURNED FACE DOWN ON THAT TABLE BY THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS THE BOOK AT ABOUT THE ELEVENTH HOUR OF DAY 520, AND NOBODY ASKED HER WHY.**

**13. THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN. THREAD, OPEN AND UNTOUCHED.** A length of new rope over the back of his chair, unused, and it was in that room on day 520 and nobody lifted it and nobody asked him anything and nobody looked at it once.

**14. THE THREE ON A FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD. THREAD, OPEN.** They went down it with a barrow each on the three hundred and ninety-first day of this flood and were one hundred and twenty-four days out at the moment Chapter 0515 prints, with that day named in the same sentence. Nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road. A party of four set out and one of the four said no before anybody asked her, and no person in any of these ten chapters counted anything about that.

**15. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT MOVE.** The eleven inherited debts carry whole and unpaid. `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md` is still not on disk. The one of four marks that came back out of place on the sheet of day 440 is still not traced. The fence and whether it stands between the right two things is not settled and was not measured. The seventh form of the calendar does not exist and was not made and the Bare Month has no length and no end and was given nothing by these ten days. Nobody got stronger and the wall said nothing on all ten days.

**16. THE ONE THING NO FILE THIS BATCH WROTE MAY PRINT.** The name of the volume before the last, and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; that volume's one rule, the panel of day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words are also reproduced at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; the day-154 sentence; the count of chapters carrying the day-154 beat, which the Volume 09 plan withdrew; AND THE WORDS THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN SPEAKS IN CHAPTER 0486. NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED, AND THE MEASUREMENT OF THE HOLD WAS NOT RE-RUN BY THIS BATCH BECAUSE RE-RUNNING IT REQUIRES PRINTING THE SPANS. NO CHAPTER OF THESE TEN CONNECTS HIS TWO ACTS, ASKS HIM ANYTHING, OR GIVES HIM A LINE TO SAY.

# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS

**At the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. The unbalanced bold mark at line three of this file is named again and was not repaired, because it is inherited and this pass did not edit a line above its own.**

**1. CLOSED BY THIS PASS.** The rag's location, which has stood in this volume as an open question between `chapter-0516.md`'s first paragraph and its last. **It is on the stone lip of the step, it has not been off that lip since the fourth hour, and it was under his hand when his hand went on the wall at the ninth hour.** The thread is closed as a fault and it is not closed as a fact: the rag goes on the stone lip on every morning of this flood and this is now unbroken across all twenty files of the volume.

**2. OPENED BY THIS PASS AND CARRIED WHOLE.** **A QUESTION FOR THE OUTLINE AND NOT FOR A BATCH: FORTY CHAPTERS WITH THE PROTAGONIST IN TWO OF THEM.** Adrian Vale is in 0512 and 0517 and in neither of them has a quoted word, is thanked, or is told he was right, and `outline/volume-11.md` §14.3 put him there. The review that triggered this pass called it the reason the batch does not move, and the answer is that it is the planned shape, so the question is carried to the next outline intact and unanswered and no chapter was changed to satisfy it.

The missing `chapter-0475.md`. The untraced mark from the sheet of day 440. **AND TWO THAT NO FICTION-LAYER PASS MAY TOUCH AND BOTH ARE LIVE: `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `phase-000-bootstrap` with `status: planned` and `attempts: 0` after twelve completed phases, and the review agent is declared `mode: subagent` and is invoked as a primary agent, so it falls back to the writer and this batch reviewed itself.** Both are controller files, both are recorded here so that a human sees them in the state layer rather than only in a log, and neither was edited.

**4. WHAT IS STILL THE ONE THING NO FILE MAY PRINT.** Unchanged. The name of the volume before the last, the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428, that volume's one rule, the panel of day 438, the answer spoken in Chapter 0357, the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, and the contents of the register's third column, which is empty and stays empty.

# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0003 — Chapters 0531 to 0540 are the next ten days

**At the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. The unbalanced bold mark at line three of this file is named again and was not repaired, because it is inherited and this batch did not edit a line above its own.**

**1. THE COUNT MADE ON FOOT. THREAD, AND IT IS THE SPINE, AND IT IS HALF CLOSED.** Four mornings, 511, 515, 517 and 521, one kind of thing, one person, four figures, and the four do not agree and are not permitted to agree. **All four stood one under another on one face of a working slate on day 522 in the order they were made. The decision to enter all four and to send one was taken out loud on day 525 after the cost was named out loud first. All four were written into the middle column of the register on day 530, one under another, the newest at the head, none struck.** What is left: **what the four are a count of has still not been said on any page or in any mouth, and the reason they differ by about a fifth is not discovered until day 532, which is not one of this batch's ten days.** There is no fifth morning and day 528 is not a morning.

**2. THE REGISTER'S MIDDLE COLUMN. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT HAS BEEN WRITTEN IN FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS VOLUME.** Five figures in it at the end of day 530, the bottom one of them the figure that went on the wall on day 490 and the four above it made on foot, *AND THE COLUMN IS RULED INTO FIVE LINES — `outline/volume-11.md:253` AND `chapter-0520.md:9-11` — SO THAT COLUMN IS FULL FROM DAY 530 AND IS FULL ON EVERY DAY AFTER IT AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME MAY PRINT A FIGURE INTO IT AGAIN.* **THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS THREAD'S OPENING SENTENCE ENDED *AND ONE RULED LINE OF ROOM LEFT BETWEEN THE OLDEST OF THE FOUR AND THE ONE THAT WAS ALREADY THERE*, AND THAT IS WITHDRAWN BY THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS; the page of Chapter 0530 printed the same line of room and was corrected, and the first edition of the page, of this thread and of the next-phase prompt are all published beside the correction.** **The top one of the five is the one that goes into the one place on the second form on day 538 and out of this city on day 545. THE COLUMN ON THE RIGHT IS EMPTY FROM THE TOP OF THE PAGE TO THE BOTTOM AND NOBODY IS ASKED ABOUT IT AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE. The person who opened the register on two of these ten days and put it back is the person who keeps it and no other hand went near that shelf.** The second slate is on that shelf with a day across the head of it and nothing under that day, it was not picked up, not turned over and not written on on any of these ten days, and **it is a hand's width further back on that shelf than it was on day 509 and nothing is under it.**

**3. THE SECOND ONE-PLACE FORM. THREAD, OPEN, AND IT IS ON A TABLE IN THAT ROOM.** A heading and one place ruled under it, and the one place is empty, and it was empty when it came up the road on day 504 and it is empty at the end of day 530. It is not the first one coming back, the two are never laid beside each other, and **the name of the thing the figure is about is on no page and in no mouth. The place is filled on day 538 with a figure and with nothing else, and there is no place on that form for the person who made the figure, and that fact has not been said out loud in any of these ten chapters.**

**4. THE STONE IN THE TROUGH. THREAD, NEW, OPEN.** A stone about the size of two fists was in the bottom of the trough at the end of that market when it came back from the near end of eleven miles of flats on day 528. Nobody in that market put it there, nobody has picked it up, the man who brought the kit back was not asked about it and did not stop to say so, and he did not put his hand into the water to find out where it came from. **Nobody has asked. It is a thread and it is not an invitation.**

**5. TWO FIGURES IN CHALK ON A STONE STEP. THREAD, NEW, OPEN, AND UNATTRIBUTED.** Four corners of the top step of the store marked on day 521, a string laid along the front edge and the back edge, two figures that came out two different ways, both written on the stone of that step in chalk, one of them carried off on the flat of a boot eleven miles of flats and the other still there. **Nobody in that market knows whose hands were on them and nobody asked, and the string on that step is gone and a length of string came off a barrow's wheel and was cut off and dropped.**

**6. THE EMPTY TIN. THREAD, OPEN.** The woman of about sixty-nine carried it up nine steps on day 523 with nothing in it and put it on a table in that room and nobody put anything in it. **She has asked three questions in this flood, a person, a fence and a line, and she did not ask a fourth on day 523 and one man in that room noticed and said nothing and nobody else noticed.**

**7. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE. THREAD, OPEN.** His board has nineteen rates on it and the space about two fingers wide in the bottom of the middle column is still not filled, and nobody has asked him what is in it. **He was right in about four seconds on day 525 about why the top one of a column is the one that goes, and on day 529 he said out loud that a wall is read across and a book is read down and that he is not going to be the man who says which one a person is meant to be using, and on day 530 he said that a price goes where the next price goes, and nobody in any of the three days was told he was right and nobody thanked him.**

**8. THE TWO MEN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR. TWO PEOPLE. NOT MERGED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** The man who keeps a stall has his board face in against his own trestle with the bare back out in the row and his own piece of chalk in his own inside breast pocket, where it has been since the two hundred and ninety-eighth day of this flood, which is the forty-third day of the Short Month, and the full form of that day is printed at full length in every chapter of these ten that carries it. **He has not been asked anything by anybody in this volume and the store's chalk in the box on that step is not his and he did not use it on any of these ten days.**

**9. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FIVE IS NOT A DESCRIPTOR IN THIS MANUSCRIPT AND NOBODY IN THESE TEN DAYS IS HIM.** The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living is in 0527 and put his hands on a ledge and opened nothing.

**10. THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN. THREAD, OPEN AND UNTOUCHED, AND NOT NAMED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** A length of new rope over the back of his chair, unused, and no hand lifted it in this batch because the man and the rope are not in any of these ten days at all.

**11. THE THREE ON A FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD. THREAD, OPEN.** They went down it with a barrow each on the three hundred and ninety-first day of this flood and were one hundred and thirty-one days out at the moment Chapter 0522 prints, with that day named in the same sentence. Nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road. A party of four set out and one of the four said no before anybody was asked her, and no person in any of these ten chapters counted anything about that.

**12. THE OLD FIGURE IN THE ROOM AND THE WALL. THREAD, OPEN AND NOT ADVANCED IN THESE TEN DAYS.** A figure on a wall in three columns' worth of space, none of the three rubbed out, the two in chalk not agreeing with each other and the wall above the board not washed on any of the fifty days. **On day 529 a man put the two surfaces in one room, a book open on a table and a board outside a window, and said out loud that one is read across and the other is read down, and said that he is not going to be the man who says which one a person is meant to be using, and that is the whole of what has happened between them and no instrument has been named.**

**13. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT MOVE.** The eleven inherited debts carry whole and unpaid. `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md` is still not on disk. The one of four marks that came back out of place on the sheet of day 440 is still not traced and no mouth in these ten days says anybody moved anything on a page. The fence and whether it stands between the right two things is not settled and was not measured and the corner in it was not measured. The days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value. The seventh form of the calendar does not exist and was not made and the Bare Month has no length and no end and was given nothing by these ten days. Nobody got stronger and the wall said nothing on all ten days. **The volume's one panel is fixed to day 540 and is unspent and no chapter of these ten carries a panel.**

**14. THE ONE THING NO FILE THIS BATCH WROTE MAY PRINT.** The name of the volume before the last, and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; that volume's one rule, the panel of day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words are also reproduced at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; the day-154 sentence; the count of chapters carrying the day-154 beat, which the Volume 09 plan withdrew; AND THE WORDS THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN SPEAKS IN CHAPTER 0486. NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED, AND THE MEASUREMENT OF THE HOLD WAS NOT RE-RUN BY THIS BATCH BECAUSE RE-RUNNING IT REQUIRES PRINTING THE SPANS. NO CHAPTER OF THESE TEN CONNECTS HIS TWO ACTS, ASKS HIM ANYTHING, OR GIVES HIM A LINE TO SAY. **THE WORDS OF VOLUME 11'S OWN ONE PANEL ARE NOT IN ANY FILE THIS BATCH WROTE AND ARE NOT DESCRIBED, AND THE FIGURE THE PLAN PUTS AT ITS OWN FEET IS NOT IN ANY FILE THIS BATCH WROTE IN ANY FORM.**

**15. TWO FIGURES A LATER WRITER MUST NOT INHERIT WRONG, RESTATED BECAUSE THE RECORD THAT RECEIVED THEM SCROLLS AWAY.** **THE FOUR MORNINGS OF THE COUNT ARE 511, 515, 517 AND 521 AND THERE IS NO FIFTH AND DAY 528 IS NOT A MORNING OF THE COUNT.** **THE STAIR AT THE BACK OF THAT ROOM IS NINE STEPS AND CHAPTER 0523 IS THE ONE CHAPTER OF THE FIFTY THAT SAYS SO, TWICE, AND EVERY OTHER CHAPTER SAYS *THAT STAIR* AND CHAPTER 0524 SAID *NINE STEPS* THREE TIMES ON THE FIRST PASS AND WAS CUT TO ZERO.**

# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS

**This block is at the foot of the file and wins over every block above it. The unbalanced bold mark at line three of this file is named again and was not repaired, because it is inherited and this pass did not edit a line above its own. The one thread line edited in place above is the register's middle column, and its first edition is published at the line itself.**

**1. THREADS THIS PASS RE-DATED, AND IT IS TWO, AND NEITHER IS CLOSED, RE-OPENED OR DISCHARGED BY THE REPAIR.**

1. **THE REGISTER'S MIDDLE COLUMN. STILL OPEN, AND IT IS A SMALLER THREAD THAN IT WAS AN HOUR AGO.** Five figures in a column ruled into five lines, entered on days 493, 530, 530, 530 and 530, the four of them on one morning in the order they were made, the bottom one of the five the figure that is on the wall. **THE COLUMN IS FULL FROM DAY 530 AND THE FULLNESS IS ITSELF THE OPEN PART: what goes in that column when the next figure of this city's own is counted on foot, and whether anybody tells anybody that there is nowhere to put it, and whether the four disagreeing figures in a full column are a thing a person in four hundred miles will ever be shown. The first one-place form is still on a page somewhere in this city with a figure in it, the second one's one place is still empty and is still filled on day 538 with a figure and nothing else, and the top one of the five is the one that goes into it and no chapter has told anybody which of the four that is.** What was withdrawn is the line of room, not the thread: the first edition of the thread said one ruled line of room was left and that is false and the first edition is published at the line above.
2. **THE COST THE REGISTER-KEEPER NAMED ON DAY 525. NEW AS A THREAD ONLY IN THE SENSE THAT IT IS NOW FIXED, AND IT IS NOT DISCHARGED.** Eleven words: *only a count made on foot can be shown a person.* **A phrase that is on a page in one mouth is settled and is not to be paraphrased, improved on, lengthened, shortened or put in a second mouth, and no later phase of this volume may move the eleven words.** Her second turn — choosing one of four does not mend the other three, and a person four hundred miles off is handed one of them and is not told there were four — is hers and is unmoved.

**2. THE THREADS THIS PASS FOUND WERE BEING DESCRIBED WRONG, AND THE CORRECTION MAKES ONE OF THEM SMALLER AND ONE OF THEM SHARPER.**

**THE WOMAN WHO WALKED A MARKET IS IN TWO OF BATCH 0003'S TEN CHAPTERS AND NOT SIX, AND THAT IS NOT A THREAD CLOSING — IT IS A THREAD SHARPENING.** Days 521 and 526. A slate under her arm and a tin in her two hands, no name, no age, no number, no descriptor, asked nothing on either day. **She is a standing open thread of this volume and not of this batch: nobody has ever asked her why she stopped, and the day map for the rest of Volume 11 does not yet promise that anybody will.** The first edition of the record and of the character file put her in six chapters and merged her with the woman of about sixty-nine, who carries the other tin and is a separate person and has asked three questions in this flood and is not going to ask a fourth, and both first editions are published where they were corrected.

**THE STORE'S CHALK IS NOT A SPENT OBJECT AND THE THREAD THAT READS IT AS ONE IS WITHDRAWN.** A stub went onto the stone of that step on day 521 and came back into the box, and the box has a thumb's length showing in it on every day of the batch including that one. **The two chalks stay a thread because neither of them is ever used and nobody ever asks the man of about thirty-four for his, and that is the volume's standing fact; what is withdrawn is the claim that the store's ran out.**

**3. WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT MOVE, AND THE LIST IS THE SAME ONE THE BATCH LEFT.** The eleven inherited debts carry whole. `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md` is still not on disk. The one of four marks that came back out of place on the sheet of day 440 is still not traced. The fence, the corner in it, and whether it stands between the right two things are not settled and were not measured. The days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value. The seventh form of the calendar does not exist and the Bare Month has no length and no end. The man of about fifty-seven and the rope are untouched and are not in any of the ten. The three questions of days 346, 396 and 445 are unanswered and the fourth is unasked. Nobody got stronger, no panel was spent, and the wall said nothing on all ten days. **And a new debt is recorded rather than paid: there is no card file for any batch of this volume after the first, and both Batch 0002 and Batch 0003 planned from the outline and from card blocks inside their own records, and this pass did not write one because a card file is an outline file.**

# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0004 — Chapters 0531 to 0540, days 531 to 540

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. The unbalanced bold mark at line three of this file is named again and was not repaired, because it is inherited and this batch did not edit a line above its own. No line above this one was deleted.**

**1. THREADS THIS BATCH ADVANCED, AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS CLOSED.**

1. **THE COUNT MADE ON FOOT. NOW SPOKEN ABOUT IN A ROOM, AND THE WORD IS ON A PAGE.** Four figures of one kind of thing, counted by the same person on four mornings at 511, 515, 517 and 521, entered in the middle column of the register in the order they were made, do not agree and are not permitted to agree. **ON DAY 532, IN THAT ROOM, A MAN SAID OUT LOUD, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS FLOOD, THAT THE FIRST OF THE FOUR AND THE LAST OF THE FOUR ARE ABOUT A FIFTH APART, THAT IT IS NOT THE WEATHER AND NOT A KNOCK AND THAT THERE IS NO ERROR IN ANY OF THE FOUR, AND HE GAVE THE THING THAT COMES ABOUT A SECOND FIGURE OF THE SAME KIND OF THING STANDING ABOVE THE FIRST ONE A NAME, AND NOBODY IN THAT ROOM SAID THAT HE WAS RIGHT, AND NOBODY IMPROVED ON IT, AND NOBODY THANKED HIM.** That name is on the page of that one chapter and in no other page of this batch and in no mouth but his on any of the ten days. **NO FIGURE OFF ANY OF THE FOUR WAS PRINTED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, NO FIFTH MORNING WAS PRINTED, AND NO CHAPTER OF THESE TEN NAMES WHAT THE FOUR ARE A COUNT OF.**
2. **THE ONE PLACE UNDER THE SECOND FORM'S HEADING. FILLED ON DAY 538 AND STILL FILLED.** The second one-place form came up that road in a satchel with a flap on day 504 and was read out on day 506, and the one place under its heading was filled on day 538 with one figure of this city made on foot and with nothing whatever else, and the day was not written beside it and no name was written on it and there is no second place under that heading and there never has been. **THE SHEET WAS FOLDED TWICE ALONG ITS CREASES AND SHUT ON THE TABLE UNDER THE ONE WINDOW ON DAY 540, WITH THE BONE LYING BESIDE IT, AND IT GOES OUT OF THIS CITY ON DAY 545 WITH THE FIGURE IN IT AND WITH NOTHING ELSE, AND NOBODY IN THIS CITY CAN BE SHOWN WHO MADE THAT FIGURE.** The thread is not closed and the form has not gone anywhere yet.
3. **THE REGISTER'S MIDDLE COLUMN. STILL FULL, AND NOW SOMEBODY HAS READ IT OUT LOUD.** Five figures in a column ruled into five lines, the top one the one that has gone onto the sheet, the bottom one the figure that is on the wall, the column on the right empty from the top of the page to the bottom on all ten of these days. **ON DAY 537 TWO PEOPLE READ THAT COLUMN DOWN FROM THE HEAD OF IT TO THE FOOT OF IT IN A ROOM, ONE READING THE FIGURES AND ONE READING THE DAYS, AND THEY DID NOT DISAGREE ABOUT A SINGLE DAY AND NEITHER OF THEM SAID SO. ON DAY 539 A MAN READ IT DOWN AGAIN AND A WOMAN READ THE DAYS AFTER HIM.** **NO FIGURE WAS PUT INTO THAT COLUMN ON ANY OF THESE TEN DAYS AND NOTHING WENT INTO THE COLUMN ON THE RIGHT ON ANY OF THEM, and the second slate was not picked up and not turned over and not written on on any of the ten, and the keeper's hand went near that shelf exactly once on each of the two days the register came off it.**
4. **THE FIGURE ON THE WALL AND THE FIGURE AT THE HEAD OF THE COLUMN. PUT IN ONE ROOM AND NOT JOINED.** On day 537 a book was stood open on a table with a wall of figures in the window below it, and about four people in that room worked out that the figure on that wall and the figure at the head of that column are of one kind of thing and about four did not, and not one of the nine said so out loud. **NO MOUTH IN ANY OF THESE TEN DAYS SAID WHICH OF THE THREE SETS OF FIGURES ON THAT BOARD IS THE ONE ANYBODY SHOULD BE READING, AND THE WALL ABOVE THAT BOARD WAS NOT WASHED ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS AND NO SECOND PERSON TOUCHED IT.**

**2. THREADS THIS BATCH FOUND THAT ARE NEW.**

**A CHAIR CARRIED UP A STAIR AND STOOD AGAINST A WALL AND NEVER USED**, and a length of new rope over the back of another chair at the other end of the same bench that has not moved all morning, and a man at the far end of that bench who was not asked anything at all. **A ROW OF NINETEEN BOARDS STANDING ON END AGAINST A WALL AT THE END OF THAT MARKET WITH ABOUT A HAND'S WIDTH OF DAYLIGHT AT THE FOOT OF THE FIFTH OF THEM**, and no person in that market knows whose hands were on that stack, and the man who moved them did not ask and was not told. **A SHEET ON A SHELF AND A SHEET ON A TABLE A TABLE'S WIDTH APART** that have not been laid beside each other and were not compared out loud on any of the three days both of them were in that room, and one of them has a figure in its one place and one of them has nothing whatever in it. **AND THE ANSWER THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE GAVE THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR ON DAY 534**, which four words long, said once, said to a man and not to a room, and not repeated by either of them on any of the days after it.

**3. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT MOVE, AND IT IS THE SAME LIST THE TWO BATCHES BEHIND IT LEFT.** The eleven inherited debts carry whole and unpaid and this batch measured none of them and repaired none of them. `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md` is still not on disk and is owed to a Volume 11 close and to no writer. The one of four marks that came back out of place on the sheet of day 440 is still not traced and no mouth in these ten days says anybody moved anything on a page. The fence is still sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and it was not measured in any of these ten days and no post was moved. The days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value in any of these ten chapters and a party of four is four. The seventh form of the calendar does not exist and was not made and the Bare Month has no length and no end and was given nothing by these ten days. Nobody got stronger. No room in any of these ten was summoned and no notice was written and the seven notices went out in Volumes 07 and 08 and this batch wrote none. **THE STAIR AT THE BACK OF THAT ROOM WAS USED ON EIGHT OF THESE TEN DAYS AND NOT COUNTED ON ANY OF THEM, AND *NINE STEPS* AND *ABOUT NINE STEPS* ARE AT ZERO IN ALL TEN FILES ON BOTH READINGS.**

**4. THE ONE THING NO FILE OF THIS BATCH WROTE MAY PRINT.** The name of the volume before the last, and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; that volume's one rule, the panel of day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words are also reproduced at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; the day-154 sentence; the count of chapters carrying the day-154 beat, which the Volume 09 plan withdrew; AND THE WORDS THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN SPEAKS IN CHAPTER 0486. # VOLUME 11, BATCH 0005 — Chapters 0541 to 0550, days 541 to 550

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. The block headed `VOLUME 11, BATCH 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS` stands above it unedited, and the unbalanced bold mark at line three of this file is named again and was not repaired, because it is inherited and this batch did not edit a line above its own. No line above this one was deleted.**

**1. WHAT THIS BATCH ADVANCED, AND EACH ONE IS THE PLAN'S OWN AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS RESOLVED.**

- **The figure that left this city.** Day 545. It went out in a satchel with a flap, with a knot in a length of string round its near corner that nobody undid and nobody asked about, and it is not withdrawn and it is not coming back. **OPEN: where it is, whether anything can reach it, and whether a figure that is outside a city can be asked anything.**
- **The wall above the store's board.** Day 542, washed once, from the top down, stopped two fingers above the figures, and not washed on any other day of the fifty. **OPEN: why it was not washed on the other forty-nine, and no mouth in any of the fifty chapters asks.**
- **The person who made the figure.** Day 547. She cannot be named by anybody in a room of nine people, and a room has been put in front of that, and nobody asked. **OPEN: everything about her, and she is not available to be asked, and a slate under an arm and a tin in two hands are the whole of what this city has.**
- **This volume's new question.** Day 548, asked a second time, to a different man, by a different person, and not answered, and the room did not hear it. **OPEN: a later volume may ask it again and may not answer it, and no chapter of this volume answers it.**
- **The reason nobody in this city can say which figure to use.** Day 550, said out loud once, by a man of about thirty-nine: one of them is in a book that reads down and the other is on a wall that reads across, and no instrument in four hundred miles can put a book and a wall in the same order. **OPEN: it is a reason and not an answer, and nothing in this volume settles it.**
- **The first cost.** Day 541, named out loud by somebody who did not pay it. **OPEN: what a room that has spent a figure is supposed to do with the three that stayed.**
- **The second cost.** Day 547, named out loud by somebody who is not the person it is about. **OPEN, and it is the volume's own subject.**
- **The knot.** Day 543, tied by a man in a room by a knot a man in a stall two along ties every morning and did not teach him, and worked at with a thumbnail by a man who ties things for a living. **OPEN: nothing. It is a knot. It is on a sheet that has left this city.**
- **The slate on the sill.** Day 549, blank, with a woman's board-foot on it. **OPEN: nothing. It is a slate with nothing on it.**
- **The handcart and the bucket.** Day 546. **OPEN: nothing. A cart went round a bucket.**

**2. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT ADVANCE, AND IT IS THE SAME LIST THE THREE PASSES BEHIND IT LEFT.** The three questions of days 346, 396 and 445 are unanswered and no fourth was asked on any of these ten days. The one of four marks that came back out of place on the sheet of day 440 is not traced and no mouth in these ten days says that anybody moved anything on any page. The fence is still sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and was not measured on either of the two days it was named. The man of about fifty-seven and the rope are untouched. The days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value and no chapter of these ten names a fourth of them as having gone. The third column of the register stays empty on all ten days and nobody asked about it and nobody is going to. The second slate is never picked up, never turned over and never written on. **Nobody got stronger and no panel was spent and THE WALL SAID NOTHING ON ALL TEN OF THESE TEN DAYS**, and there is no eighth notice and none was written.

THE FIGURE IN THE ONE PLACE UNDER THE HEADING ON THE SHEET THAT CAME UP THE ROAD ON DAY 552 IS PRINTED NOWHERE, AND IT IS STILL A FIGURE OF THIS CITY, AND NOBODY HAS SAID OUT LOUD WHAT IT IS A FIGURE OF.** It is named as present and as not written out on any page in four hundred miles in Chapters 0563, 0568 and 0570, and **not one of the three prints it and no mouth in any of the three says what it counts.** It has been on a table since day 552 and has not been filled in, withdrawn, compared with anything or sent anywhere. *Open, and it belongs to the volume's own decision day and not to any day this batch wrote.*

**2. THE FIGURE ON THE SIDE OF THE TALLY-BOARD AT THE WHARF THAT THE SUN DOES NOT GET TO IS PRINTED NOWHERE, AND THE MAN IT IS ABOUT IS IN NONE OF DAYS 561 TO 570 AND WAS ASKED NOTHING.** It is referenced in Chapters 0567, 0568 and 0570 only as a figure that went out of this city in a satchel with a flap, and **no chapter of these ten prints it, no chapter prints it as the same figure as anything, and no chapter says where it came from.** The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf is in none of these ten chapters and is not asked anything in any of them. *Open.*

**3. THE THIRD ONE-PLACE FORM HAS NOT BEEN WRITTEN INTO, HAS NOT BEEN CLOSED AND HAS NOT LEFT THIS CITY.** It lay on a table in Chapters 0563, 0568 and 0570 with its one place showing a figure of this city and nothing under that figure anywhere on it, and **not one line of words was written under any figure on any surface in this city on any of these ten days, and no surface in this city carried one, and the sheet did not go anywhere.** *Open, and the writing and the going are both on later days and neither is this batch's.*

**4. THE REGISTER'S RIGHT-HAND COLUMN IS EMPTY ON ALL TEN OF THESE DAYS AND NOBODY HAS ASKED WHAT IT IS FOR.** It runs the whole height of the page and there is nothing whatever in it from the top of the page to the bottom in Chapters 0563, 0567, 0568, 0569 and 0570, and **the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register did not look at it in Chapter 0568 and nobody in any of the five put a word in it and nobody in any of the five was asked.** *Open, and it is a decision not to, and it is not this batch's to take.*

**5. THE SECOND SLATE IS ON ITS SHELF WITH A DAY ACROSS THE HEAD OF IT AND NOTHING UNDER IT, AND IT WAS NOT PICKED UP AND NOT TURNED OVER AND NOT WRITTEN ON ON ANY OF THESE TEN DAYS.** It is declared in Chapters 0563, 0567, 0568, 0569 and 0570 and **no hand but the keeper's went near that shelf on any of the ten, and in two of the five the keeper's hand is named as the one that did not touch it.** The word on it is not printed by any file this batch wrote. *Open, and what is on it is not named anywhere.*

**6. THE NAME THAT WAS TAKEN OFF THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE'S OWN BOARD IN HIS OWN HAND ABOUT NINE YEARS AGO IS STILL NOT ON THE BOARD AND STILL HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED FOR.** Chapter 0564 carries the space about two fingers wide and carries that he has not said so to anybody, and **nobody in that chapter asked him what was in it and no chapter of this batch printed the name and no chapter asked him about it.** The volume's own new question is put to him on a later day and is about a person in a column and not about that name, and no chapter may join the two. *Open.*

**7. THE THREE WHO WENT DOWN THE FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD ON DAY 391 ARE ON THAT ROAD AND NOBODY IN THIS CITY CAN BE SHOWN ONE PERSON ON IT, AND NO CHAPTER OF DAYS 561 TO 570 PRINTS HOW FAR OUT THEY ARE.** They are named in Chapters 0562, 0566 and 0569 with the day they went named in the same sentence, and **the count of days since is printed in none of the three, because the plan holds two figures for it at the ends of the volume and neither row is corrected and no chapter of these ten needs either.** The man of about fifty-nine was in a room in Chapter 0569 and was not asked about them and did not offer it. *Open, and the figure is still disputed between two rows of the plan.*

**8. WHETHER THE FENCE STANDS BETWEEN THE RIGHT TWO THINGS IS A QUESTION, AND IT IS STILL A QUESTION, AND IT IS ASKED IN NO CHAPTER OF THESE TEN.** The fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies in Chapters 0562 and 0566, it is not measured in either, no post is moved in either, no withy is bound in either, and the corner nobody has taken a cord to is the corner in Chapter 0566. **Nobody asked the man of about fifty-seven anything in either chapter and the length of new rope over the back of his chair is named in Chapter 0566 and is not used, not cut, not taken up, not lifted and not carried off that chair by any hand.** *Open, and the fifth volume running has not settled it.*

**9. THE SECOND OF THE VOLUME'S THREE DISCOVERIES AND ITS THIRD ARE ON A PAGE, AND THE FOURTH IS NOT AND IS NOT THIS BATCH'S.** Chapter 0563 is about a shape, that one ruled place and one ruled column being the same thing ruled twice. Chapter 0568 is about kind, that a figure that came down the road and a figure that went up it are the same kind of number with no instrument in this city able to put the two in one order. **NEITHER WAS AGREED WITH OUT LOUD, NEITHER WAS IMPROVED ON, NEITHER WAS THANKED, AND IN BOTH THE PERSON WHO KEEPS THE REGISTER WAS IN THE ROOM AND SAID NOTHING.** *Open, and the fourth is later.*

**10. THE TWO HALVES OF THE STORE'S BOARD DO NOT AGREE WITH EACH OTHER AND NOBODY CAN SAY WHICH OF THE THREE SETS OF FIGURES ON IT IS THE ONE ANYBODY SHOULD BE READING.** Chapters 0564, 0565, 0567 and 0568 carry that board with three sets of figures on it, none rubbed out, and not one of the three carrying a line of words under it, and about four people at that end of that market read one of them and go on every morning and about four do not and the two groups have never said a word to each other about which one they read. *Open, and the fifth volume running.*

**11. THE COUNT MADE ON FOOT WAS MADE ON FOUR MORNINGS AND THE FOUR DO NOT AGREE AND NO FIFTH MORNING EXISTS.** Chapter 0561 names all four with the day each was made in the same chapter and says there has not been a fifth and there is not going to be one, and **nobody counted anything on that ground on that day and there was nothing on that ground for anybody to count.** *Open, and the four are the four.*

**12. THE THIRTY-SIX WRONG-WORD DUPLICATE RUNS AND THE SEVENTY-FIVE NARRATOR-FRAME FILES ARE A NEW FINDING AND THEY ARE DEBTS, AND THIS BATCH PAID SIX OF THE FIRST ON ITS OWN PAGES AND NONE OF THE SECOND.** The forty-word duplication row is published in full with both files and the length at `state/batch-summaries/volume-12-batch-0002.md` §4.1, and twenty-seven of the runs stood on this batch's own ten files and were repaired, and the run that did not is between `chapter-0560.md` and `chapter-0567.md` and the 0560 half belongs to Batch 0001 and was not touched. **Outside Volume 12 there are four thousand five hundred and ninety runs of forty words or more in one hundred and thirty-seven files and the longest is two hundred and seventeen words, and every one of them is inherited and is repaired by nobody and is rerouted by nobody.** The narrator-frame family stands in seventy-five files across the manuscript, sixty-one of them in Volume 01, and six of the seventy-five were this batch's own and were repaired and one is `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0555.md`, which this batch published and did not touch. *Open and routed to a review repair pass, and it is the largest structural debt this repository has and no batch phase owns it.*

**THE ELEVEN INHERITED DEBTS AND THE SIX NAMED AT `outline/volume-12.md` §21 ARE CARRIED FORWARD WHOLE AND UNPAID. NONE WAS TAKEN, NONE WAS REPAIRED AND NONE WAS REROUTED BY THIS BATCH, INCLUDING THE FOUR MISSING CARD FILES OF VOLUME 11 AND `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md`, WHICH DOES NOT EXIST AND WHICH A BATCH MAY NAME AND MAY NOT WRITE, SUMMARISE, DECIDE ANYTHING IN, OR ROUTE. THE FIGURE THE PLAN HOLDS FOR THE STATE LAYER IS IN NO CHAPTER AND IN NO FILE THIS BATCH WROTE.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-12/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`.**

**AND THE REPAIR PASS ON THE BATCH BEHIND THIS BLOCK OPENED NO THREAD AND CLOSED NONE, AND THE TWO THINGS IT ADDED ARE BOTH ABOUT THE PROSE AND NEITHER IS A STORY THREAD. First, seven sentences across the ten chapters of days 561 to 570 were word-for-word copies of sentences in earlier chapters of the same volume, and the motifs they carried — the chalk date on the door, the census of about four, the attribution tag — were kept and reworded instead, so the motifs stand and the copies are gone. Second, a garbled sentence in 0565 was rewritten, the repeat measure was cut back and the possessive was thinned. A reader of this file who is tracking what the volume is ABOUT will find nothing in that pass, and that is the correct result: no thread was opened, none was closed, and no open question moved.**

**AND THE TWO FINDINGS A REVIEW RAISED THAT THIS FILE DOES NOT CARRY AS THREADS, BECAUSE NEITHER IS A STORY THREAD AND BOTH ARE PUBLISHED HERE SO THAT A LATER PHASE CANNOT MISTAKEN THEM FOR SETTLED. Volumes 01 to 04 hold a large quantity of recycled prose and Volumes 02 and 03 are the worst: a single forty-word passage recurs in thirty-eight of Volume 02's fifty chapters and in forty-two of Volume 03's, and two adjacent chapters of Volume 02 share a run of two hundred and seventeen words. This was routed before this batch and this batch neither paid it nor took it, and a repair pass that quietly reopened four published volumes to settle a figure it did not raise would be the failure this repository has paid for five times. It is an inherited debt and it is unpaid and it is a publication exposure. Separately, `state/chapter-summaries.md` was given the missing entry for days 561 to 570, so the next batch has chapter-level story for the ten days it continues from, and the forward-looking warning in the BATCH 0001 block of that file addressed to "a writer of days 561 to 570" is spent and is not to be carried as a live briefing.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-12/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`.**

## VOLUME 12, BATCH 0003 — newest block in this file. A batch wrote it. It opened no thread and it closed none.

**A BATCH DOES NOT DECIDE WHAT A THREAD IS. THIS BLOCK RECORDS WHAT MOVED ON TEN DAYS AND WHAT DID NOT, AND IT OPENS NOTHING.**

**WHAT MOVED, AND EACH ITEM IS A MOVEMENT OF AN EXISTING THREAD AND NOT A NEW ONE.**

1. **THE LINE OF WORDS UNDER THE FIGURE, THREAD ALREADY OPEN, MOVED ONCE.** It was agreed to on the two hundred and sixtieth day of the Bare Month, in daylight, in a room with a date in chalk on the door, out loud, by the man whose figure it is going under, after he named the cost out loud first in his own mouth. It is not written. The one place under the heading still shows the figure that came down that road. Nobody wrote a word under anything on any of the ten days of this batch, and not one surface in this city carries a line of words under a figure.
2. **THE TALLY-BOARD AT THE WHARF, THREAD ALREADY OPEN, MOVED ONCE AND IT CANNOT GO BACK.** It is face up on the top of its own stack. It was turned by the man who owns it, with his own hand, and nobody in that yard said a word about it. The figure on it is printed nowhere, nobody in this city has been shown where it came from, nobody has asked, and about four people a day walk past that yard and not one of them stops.
3. **THE THREAD OF WHAT THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT'S FIGURE IS ABOUT, ALREADY OPEN, DID NOT MOVE.** Nobody in any of the ten chapters can say what that figure is a figure of, nobody asked him where it came from, and the chapter that carries his decision says in narration that being in the room did not help. It is open and it is open wider than it was.
4. **THE THREAD OF THE SECOND SLATE AND THE EMPTY COLUMN, ALREADY OPEN, DID NOT MOVE.** She wiped the shelf in front of nine people and did not touch the slate. She looked at the column on the right of that page four times and said nothing about it four times. Nobody in this city has ever asked her what that column is for and nobody in any of the ten chapters did, and this is the day before the one on which a man is going to look at it.
5. **THE THREAD OF THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE'S OWN BOARD AND THE NAME THAT IS NOT ON IT, ALREADY OPEN, DID NOT MOVE.** A woman in that room said out loud on day 574 that he took it off himself and that nobody is going to ask him, and he was not asked in any of the ten chapters and the space is still about two fingers wide. The volume's new question is not put to him until a day that is not one of these ten.
6. **THE THREAD OF THE SEVEN SHEETS AND THE ABSENT EIGHTH, ALREADY OPEN, WAS NAMED ONCE AND DID NOT MOVE.** A room counted the seven that went out of this city in this flood off the fingers of two hands, got two numbers, settled neither, and nobody wrote on the door.

**AND WHAT DID NOT MOVE, WHICH IS THE LONGER LIST.** Which of the three sets of figures on the store's board is the one anybody should be reading. The one of four marks that came back out of place on the sheet of day 440. The three questions of days 346, 396 and 445, and the questions of days 498 and 548, and the fourth the woman of about sixty-nine did not ask, for the fifth volume running. The fence, and whether it stands between the right two things. The man of about fifty-seven and his length of new rope. The length of time the second slate has been standing. The Bare Month's length and its end, and the seventh form of the calendar, which does not exist and was not made. The three who went down the road, and the number of days out, which is printed nowhere and which the plan holds two figures for. The person with a slate under an arm and a tin in two hands, and nobody asking her why she stopped. The two men of about thirty-four are two people. The two men of about thirty-eight are two people. The days.

**AND WHAT A NEXT BATCH SHOULD TAKE FROM ALL OF IT. The ten cards at the head of `state/batch-summaries/volume-12-batch-0003.md` stand and were written before Chapter 0571 existed, and the ten chapters stand, and the plan is unchanged and the days stand. The instruments came back clean at forty words on all three passes and at sentence scale, and what the repair of this batch's own drafts actually found was that the house's motifs are easy to copy as sentences. The motifs of this volume — the date in chalk on the door with no name under it, the census of about nine and about four, the fence and the corner nobody has taken a cord to, the attribution tag, the declaration of a full column beside an empty one — may be used freely, and must be worded differently each time, because the difference between a motif and a copy is the wording. The one thing a writer of days 581 to 590 must not do is write a line of words under anything before the day fixed for it, and one thing it must not miss is the day the wall says something, which is not a day anybody gets a second time.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`.**

## VOLUME 12, BATCH 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS. Newest block in this file. No thread was opened, closed, narrowed or rerouted.

**NO STORY THREAD MOVED AND NONE IS ADDED.** The one item this adds is a measurement owed by nobody and measurable by the next batch: **137 of the 184 sentences in Chapters 0571 to 0580 carry a run of nine words or more reproduced verbatim from an earlier chapter file, mostly the §20.7 register declarations in the register's own phrases.** It is not zero, it was never run, it is not repaired, and a later batch may measure it but may not settle it by rewriting declarations in chapters it did not write. **The three corrected figures named in `state/continuity.md` and in §14 of the batch record are corrections to what this batch claimed, not changes to what happened in the ten days.** Day 578 is still a day that was written and not a day the words of were permitted; the held things are still held and were neither printed nor certified absent; the eleven inherited debts, the six named at §21.1, and the three publication debts are all carried forward exactly as the batch carried them and this pass took none of them.
